# 00 — Parity Inventory (master checklist)

**Scope.** Every user-facing feature of the current PyMOL front-end (Qt desktop GUI + the GUI that PyMOL draws inside its own OpenGL viewport) that the React web client must reproduce. This document is the **definition of done**. A feature missing here is a feature missing in the product.

**Target architecture assumed throughout** (report contradictions, do not silently deviate):

- PyMOL C++/Python backend stays. It runs as one local Python process, one browser client, localhost, full filesystem access.
- A Python bridge service exposes the `cmd` API + events over WebSocket/HTTP.
- The 3D viewport renders **client-side in WebGL (three.js)**, drawing geometry PyMOL has **already computed** (surface/cartoon triangle meshes, CGO buffers) and serialized over the wire. three.js must **not** recompute representations from atoms.
- All Qt dialogs/panels/menus and PyMOL's viewport-internal GUI become React components.

## How to use this document

1. Every row is a unit of parity work with an unchecked box. Do not check a box until the Acceptance column of the owning work package is satisfied against the Qt build running side-by-side.
2. `Source ref` is a real `file:line` a mapper read. When implementing, open that file first. Do not invent APIs; if an API is not named here, grep for it and say so.
3. `Backend contract` is what the bridge must expose. Entries marked **NEW** do not exist today and are a backend task, not a React task.
4. Full behavioural detail (exact strings, exact pixel math, every argument) lives in the per-area doc named in each section header. This inventory preserves the enumerations; the per-area docs preserve the prose.

## Legend

- Complexity: `trivial` / `moderate` / `hard` / `very-hard` — as assessed by the area mapper.
- **NEW** in Backend contract = an API that does not exist in this tree and must be added to the bridge (Python) or to C++.
- **BROKEN** = the feature exists in the Qt UI but does not work in this open-source build.
- ⛔ = at risk from the client-side-WebGL decision; cross-referenced in [§13 Not achievable client-side](#13-not-achievable-client-side).

| [x] | **Command line drag-enter live preview** — dragEnterEvent inserts dropped text at the cursor and selects it; first URL that is a local file inserts `url.toLocalFile()` instead of raw text; saves `_saved_pos`/`_saved_text`; dragLeaveEvent restores original text and cursor; dropEvent accepts the proposed action; dragMoveEvent is a deliberate no-op | `modules/pmg_qt/pymol_qt_gui.py:1093` | none (pure UI); result goes through `cmd.do` on submit | HTML5 dragenter/dragleave/drop reproducing preview-insert then restore-on-leave. File drops: use path when available (`text/uri-list`), else upload to the bridge and insert the returned temp path | moderate | **[wave 4] dragenter over #command_line with `text/uri-list: file:///tmp/my%20file.pdb` at cursor 5 of `load ` produced `load /tmp/my file.pdb` with selection [5,21] (toLocalFile path preferred, inserted run SELECTED); dragleave restored `load ` with the cursor at 5; drop inserted for real; dragover is a no-op except preventDefault. GAP: for a drag originating OUTSIDE the page the HTML5 data store is in protected mode, so there is no live preview — the drop still inserts.** **[wave 8] NOT ATTEMPTED this wave and the gap stands as written. The clause names a browser platform limit, not a defect: during dragenter/dragover from OUTSIDE the page the HTML5 data store is in protected mode, so dataTransfer.types lists text/uri-list but getData returns '', and Qt's insert-then-select preview cannot be reproduced for the one case that matters most (a file dragged from Finder). Deprioritised against the four rows whose gap was a missing cross-feature seam. If it is ever to be closed it needs a decision, not code: either accept a placeholder preview (insert a marker of unknown length, which Qt does not do) or record the row as achievable only for in-page drags.** **[wave 9] CLOSED, by taking the decision the wave-8 note said this row needed, and the clause it rests on turns out to be half wrong. The clause is right that the PAYLOAD is withheld during dragenter and wrong that this leaves nothing to preview: dataTransfer.types and dataTransfer.items[i].kind ARE readable in protected mode, so the browser will say 'one file' or 'some text' even while it refuses to say which. dragPreview.ts now exports protectedPlaceholder(), and a drag from outside the page previews a stand-in that is inserted at the cursor and SELECTED like Qt's, restored on leave like Qt's, and REPLACED by the real path on drop. Measured in features/console/p9a1drag.dom.test.tsx against a DataTransfer modelling a real Finder drag as Chromium presents it (types ['Files','text/uri-list'], one item of kind 'file', getData '' for everything, files EMPTY): 'load ' with the cursor at 5 becomes 'load ⟨file⟩' selected [5,11]; dragleave restores 'load ' with the cursor at 5; the drop, where everything becomes readable at once, gives 'load /tmp/my file.pdb' with the caret at 21 - replacing the stand-in, not appending to it. THE DEVIATION, stated rather than hidden: Qt inserts the true text because Qt can read it, and the file's NAME is withheld until drop in every engine, so the inserted run has the wrong length. That is the whole of what the platform allows, and the purpose of the Qt behaviour - see where the drop will land before letting go, and get your line back if you change your mind - is preserved. A drag from INSIDE the page is untouched: getData answers, the real text is previewed ('load /tmp/1ubq.pdb', selection [5,18]) and no placeholder appears. Files count comes from items, not files, because files is empty until drop; a Files type with an empty item list (Safari) still previews. ONE DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED IN PASSING: applyPreview consumed the saved state and returned false without restoring, so a drop that turned out to carry nothing - or a failed upload - left the stand-in sitting in the command line; it now puts the line back. 8 tests; the mutation that removes the placeholder turned 2 red. [wave 9 AUDIT] CONFIRMED as a legitimate closure of a gap that explicitly asked for a DECISION rather than code ('either accept a placeholder preview ... or record the row as achievable only for in-page drags'). The decision is taken, the deviation from Qt is stated rather than hidden, and the in-page path is left untouched and separately asserted. The incidental fix - applyPreview consuming the saved state and returning false without restoring, which left the stand-in in the command line after a drop that carried nothing - is a real defect that only building this could have surfaced. Depth of my check: suite re-run, no mutation of my own on this row.**

What is genuinely missing is that the two consumers this row names do not subscribe to that tap: features/menubar re-reads a menu's settings on OPEN and 120 ms after each click (deliberate, documented in MenuBar.tsx), and shell/AppShell.tsx polls cmd.get('session_file') at 1 Hz for document.title instead of reacting to index 440. Both are 'a poll instead of a push', not 'no sync'.** **[wave 9] CLOSED. The gap named exactly two consumers that did not subscribe to the settings tap, and both now do, through one new shell-owned module: apps/web/src/shell/settingsTap.ts. It is Qt's own shape (update_feedback reports WHICH INDICES changed, the consumer then reads those settings back), not a store subscription, and it is shell-owned rather than a getSettingsService import because the shell must not depend on features/settings being installed. FOUR THINGS MEASURED OVER THE SOCKET FIRST (bridge/tests/test_p9_shell.py, 6 tests). (1) cmd.do(line, echo=0) installs panels.settings with NO console line, while the same line as a {t:'do'} frame prints PyMOL>/import tenmol_bridge.panels.settings as _s;_s.install() - so the shell installing a tap at startup costs the user nothing to read; the mutation echo=1 turns that test red. (2) tenmol_settings_drain takes the cursor as an ARGUMENT, so two consumers with two cursors BOTH saw index 23 after one set orthoscopic and ended on the same cursor - which is what makes a second consumer legal beside features/settings' one, where a second reader of cmd.get_setting_updates would see nothing. (3) cmd.set session_file surfaces as index 440 and reads back as [440, '/tmp/p9-a1-title.pse', '/tmp/p9-a1-title.pse']. (4) tenmol_settings_values resolves NAMES and SILENTLY DROPS unresolvable ones - 3 rows for 4 names - which is why the client resolver falls back to one call per name when the batch comes back short. install() also leaves catalogueBuilt false, so the shell pays for a drain, not for ~1000 settings. CLIENT, 14 tests in shell/p9a1tap.dom.test.tsx and features/menubar/p9a1push.dom.test.tsx. document.title is now watch(440): a session_file change nobody typed in the browser retitles the window, and once the tap is live the 1 Hz cmd.get('session_file') stops entirely - the call count is FROZEN across 2.2 s of the shell interval, and the title still follows, including back to bare 'PyMOL'. A full batch re-reads 440 even when the batch does not name it. With the module refusing to install (3 attempts, then the poller stops) the 1 Hz poll is still there and still titles the window, so nothing was traded away. The menu bar re-reads an OPEN menu when a batch names one of ITS indices: Display's Orthoscopic View tick moves with nobody clicking, an unrelated index costs no round trip, nothing is read while every menu is closed, and the 120 ms post-click re-read is no longer armed while the tap is live (it remains the fallback when there is no tap). Five mutations - drop the tap.live guard, ignore full batches, always arm the 120 ms timer, drop the index filter, drop the push - each turned 1 to 3 tests red. [wave 9 AUDIT] CONFIRMED. The gap named exactly two consumers and both now read the tap. The claim that most needed checking - that the 1 Hz cmd.get('session_file') stops entirely once the tap is live, rather than running beside it - is enforced by a real assertion: removing `if (tap.live) return;` from useWindowTitle's tick reddens exactly one test ('follows the tap, with no cmd.get poll once the tap is live') and leaves the other seven green, which is the right blast radius for that one guard. These are real-timer tests (the file takes 6.3 s of wall clock, not fake timers), so the frozen call count is observed across genuine elapsed time. The bridge half passes alone (test_p9_shell.py 6 passed) and the degradation path - three failed installs, then the poller keeps the title - is tested too, so nothing was traded away.**

The clause says the Stereo Mode leaves 'will issue stereo anaglyph\|quadbuffer\|byrow\|openvr that the WebGL viewport cannot honour'. That premise only holds for Mode G. This client has TWO viewport modes (features/viewport/ViewportPanel.tsx: 'Mode P: server-rendered' with an automatic fallback to it from Mode G), and in Mode P PyMOL renders the scene itself through a real GL context - the bridge banner on every run of the test suite reads 'engine running: PyMOL 3.2.0a, 800x600, gl=Apple M4 Max' - and the browser only displays the pixels. So the software stereo modes (anaglyph, crosseye, walleye, byrow, chromadepth) would plausibly arrive as pixels and WORK in Mode P, and the correct client behaviour may be to leave them live and refuse only quadbuffer and openvr. That is a plausibility from source and from the bridge banner, NOT an observation: I did not run any stereo command. The reason is unchanged from wave 8 and still governs - stereo is global, quadbuffer asks a context that has no quad buffers and openvr reaches for a VR runtime, so measuring it in the shared engine can take the one process down and with it every other agent's tests; it needs the subprocess pattern (bridge/tests/test_f7_startup.py::_subprocess), and a subprocess PyMOL is headless, which is NOT the product's GL configuration, so even that would not settle Mode P. Until someone measures it against a GL-backed engine the leaves stay live and unguarded, exactly as the row says.** **[wave 10] THE MEASUREMENT WAVES 8 AND 9 CALLED IMPOSSIBLE IS TAKEN, and their risk assessment is falsified. All nine Stereo Mode leaves were issued against a GL-BACKED bridge in its own process (the e2e harness's, so a crash would have cost nothing): anaglyph, crosseye, walleye, byrow, chromadepth, swap, quadbuffer, openvr, off. EVERY one replied ok in under 2 ms, the engine stayed running, and healthz.draws kept climbing by ~89 per 1.5 s (about 59 fps) throughout, ending at 969 draws with state running. quadbuffer and openvr do not reach anything dangerous: PyMOL refuses them itself (" Error: no 'quadbuffer' support detected (force with 'pymol -S')" and " Error: 'openvr' stereo mode not available in this build") and changes no state - there is no VR runtime call and no quad-buffer allocation to survive. The software modes DO take effect: stereo on with stereo_mode 10 anaglyph, 2 crosseye, 3 walleye, 6 byrow; chromadepth leaves stereo off. So wave 9's plausibility is now a fact: in Mode P these arrive as pixels and work, and the row's premise that "the WebGL viewport cannot honour them" holds only for Mode G. STILL OPEN, and now a one-line product decision rather than an unmeasurable one: in Mode G the leaves change the server-side setting and nothing visible happens, because the browser renders geometry itself - so the Stereo Mode submenu should be hidden or annotated when the viewport is in Mode G, and that is features/menubar plus features/viewport, neither of which is in this agent's directories. WHAT WOULD SETTLE THE REST: nothing further needs measuring; it needs the menubar owner.** **[wave 11] DECIDED, IMPLEMENTED AND PROVED - the one-line product decision wave 10 left for the menubar owner is taken, and the cross-directory blocker it named is gone. THE RULE: a stereo mode is available when BOTH EYES FIT IN ONE 2-D IMAGE, and it is visible for exactly the reps the server is still drawing. So the submenu is neither deleted nor hidden. quadbuffer and openvr render DISABLED with a TRANSPORT-level reason: the bridge's frame path is one glReadBuffer(GL_COLOR_ATTACHMENT0) off a single offscreen FBO (framestream.py:905) encoded as one image per frame, so there is no second buffer to carry the second eye and no HMD at the far end of a WebSocket. That reason survives a pymol -S build; PyMOL's own refusals are quoted as corroboration, not as the reason. The other seven stay live and still issue their literal line. Every live leaf's tooltip now names what it does AND where it will be seen, from a LIVE read of _bridge.render_stats().modeP.params.geometryReps - the list the compositor declares on _bridge.set_pixel_stream (wiring.ts:125, filtered on effective==='geometry'), i.e. exactly 'the reps this browser draws itself'. That answers the Mode-G half without features/viewport publishing anything. After a click the client reads stereo and stereo_mode back and writes one console line, because {t:'do'} answers ok even when PyMOL rejected the line. MEASURED AGAINST THE ENGINE (bridge/tests/test_p11_menus.py, with stereo, stereo_mode, chromadepth and stereo_shift saved by value and restored): anaglyph on/10, crosseye on/2, walleye on/3, byrow on/6; chromadepth turns stereo OFF and sets chromadepth 1 (Executive.cpp:9548 - the label lies, it is not a stereo mode); swap negates stereo_shift and touches neither setting; quadbuffer and openvr change nothing and print their own error; stereo off leaves stereo_mode latched. All nine reply ok in under 50 ms and the engine keeps drawing (healthz draws climbing, state running). MEASURED IN A REAL BROWSER at 1280x900: all nine leaves render in tree order, Quad-Buffered Stereo and OpenVR are the only two disabled, elementFromPoint lands inside every row, and clicking Anaglyph produced the console line ' stereo anaglyph: stereo on, stereo_mode 10 - the server is drawing the whole scene (Mode P), so this applies to all of it'. A PRE-EXISTING DEFECT HAD TO BE FIXED FIRST OR NONE OF THIS REACHED A USER: .menu's overflow-y:auto made CSS compute overflow-x:visible to auto, so the submenu was clipped and NEVER PAINTED - parent scrollWidth 418 against clientWidth 208, elementFromPoint over the rows returning CANVAS, and a screenshot showing the Stereo Mode row highlighted with nothing beside it. MenuList now positions nested lists position:fixed; 684 leaves, 11 menus, 4 levels, 0 unreachable. Tests: p11stereo.test.ts pins the table against the harvested submenu and against stereo_dict (constants.py:130-137); p11stereoMenu.dom.test.tsx pins the rendered menu, the two refusals with their sentences, the tooltip changing with the live geometryReps answer, and the console readback. MUTATION-TESTED: dropping the UNAVAILABLE_COMMANDS check, the annotate prop, its recursion into submenus, or the readback each turns 1-3 red. WHAT IS DELIBERATELY NOT CLAIMED: the scope sentence uses the CLIENT's declaration, not the bridge's mask plan (render_stats().modeP.mask.rasterizing / visibleReps), so if the bridge refuses a mask and keeps rasterising a declared rep, that rep is in stereo underneath the browser's own monoscopic geometry - a refinement, not the named gap. [wave 11 AUDIT] CONFIRMED, including the half that IS the product decision - the Mode-G annotation - which I drove myself rather than read. In Chromium at 1280x900 with everything in Mode P, all nine tooltips end 'the server is drawing the whole scene (Mode P), so this applies to all of it'. Switching sticks to Mode G through the viewport HUD's own G button changes every LIVE leaf, with no reload, to '... sticks is drawn by this browser (Mode G) and will NOT be in stereo', and clicking Anaglyph then wrote ' stereo anaglyph: stereo on, stereo_mode 10 - sticks is drawn by this browser (Mode G) and will NOT be in stereo; switch those reps back to P in the viewport HUD to see them in stereo' into the console pane. Exactly two leaves render disabled (Quad-Buffered Stereo and OpenVR), both carrying the TRANSPORT reason with PyMOL's own refusal quoted only as corroboration, and all nine hit-test to themselves via elementFromPoint. Mutations: changing one expected stereo_mode in the bridge table (byrow 6 -> 5) turns test_stereo_leaves_do_exactly_what_the_client_table_says red; removing the annotate lookup in MenuList turns 3 of 13 p11stereoMenu tests red. Shared-global hygiene checked in the source and by running the full suite: stereo, stereo_mode, chromadepth and stereo_shift are all saved BY VALUE and restored in a finally, and the 1839-test bridge suite shows no fallout. The declared non-claim (the sentence uses the CLIENT's declaration, not the bridge's mask plan) is the right scope.**

THE COLOUR SPACE IS PyMOL'S OWN, which is the only thing that makes such a picker more than decoration: hsv.ts is a transcription of Lib/colorsys.py, the module modules/pymol/viewing.py:1971 imports for spectrum interpolation=hsv, so the H slider walks the same path between two colours that PyMOL walks. It is pinned ACROSS BOTH LANGUAGES with no private copy of the answers: __fixtures__/p9a1hsv.json holds 15 round-trip and 9 forward vectors, bridge/tests/test_p9_shell.py prints colorsys' answers for them INSIDE the running engine and asserts they match to 1e-12, and p9a1hsv.test.ts asserts the TypeScript matches the same file - break either implementation and one of the two suites goes red. Two transcription details that a clean rewrite silently changes are pinned by name: Python's % never returns a negative so magenta is h=0.8333333333333334 and not -1/6 (a bare JS % puts every downstream slider at its minimum), and grey is h=0,s=0 by minc===maxc. That last one is why the picker HOLDS the hue while it is open: drag V to 0 and the colour is grey, rgbToHsv reports hue 0, and a picker that re-derived H each render would bring a blue swatch back up as red - measured, 240 deg survives a trip through black and returns rgb(0,0,255). The draft is dropped the moment the colour arrives from the name field, the list or the hex box. Everything downstream still agrees: hsv(210,50,80) lands on the spinboxes' 2-decimal grid as 0.4/0.6/0.8 with the swatch at rgb(102,153,204), and Apply still submits set_color p9a1_green, [0.00, 1.00, 0.00] then recolor in one cmd.do. 15 tests (8 unit, 7 DOM); three mutations - drop the held hue, keep a stale hue on a name change, and a hand-written hue strip - each went red. The wave-6 judgement that a virtualised list is unnecessary at 178 names stands. [wave 9 AUDIT] CONFIRMED. The one item wave 8 left (the HSV/RGB picker) exists, is inside features/colors, and toggles the trio rather than adding a second one. hsv.ts is a faithful transcription of Lib/colorsys.py including the two details that matter - the `((h % 1) + 1) % 1` normalisation Python's % gives for free, and grey decided by minc === maxc - and the cross-language pin is genuine: __fixtures__/p9a1hsv.json is read by BOTH p9a1hsv.test.ts and bridge/tests/test_p9_shell.py, which prints colorsys' answers for the same vectors inside the running engine, so neither side owns the expectations. The held-hue behaviour is not decorative: replacing `hsvDraft ?? rgbToHsv(rgb)` with `rgbToHsv(rgb)` reddens one test. No colour-table damage: the bridge half only imports colorsys and registers nothing, so the shared-process colour hazard is not touched.**

BridgeServer tracks client liveness on the 10 Hz status thread (_check_client_liveness): the first connection ARMS it, a disconnect starts the clock, a reconnect disarms it, and after idle_shutdown_seconds it calls request_shutdown with a reason - so 'only cmd.quit sets shutdownRequested' is no longer true. Two layers, and only the second is new: a CLOSED tab sends a WebSocket close and the endpoint's finally removes the session at once; a SUSPENDED machine sends nothing and uvicorn's ws_ping_interval/ws_ping_timeout (20 s each) closes the socket instead - either way the observable is len(sessions)==0, which is why a client-side approximation could never have worked. /healthz gained liveness {clients, clientsEver, idleSeconds, idleShutdownSeconds, armed} and shutdownReason. Four tests in bridge/tests/test_p10_infra.py, one of which WAITS THE GRACE OUT with it turned down to 0.4 s and asserts the reason text, and one that proves it never arms before a client has ever connected (mutation: deleting the _clients_ever guard fails 2 tests). STILL OPEN, precisely and in another owner's file: idle_shutdown_seconds defaults to 0 (off) because pnpm dev reloads the page constantly and this suite shares one engine across long client-free stretches, so it takes TENMOL_BRIDGE_IDLE_SHUTDOWN or a launcher setting it; and bridge/tenmol_bridge/__main__.py:156 calls uvicorn.run() and never reads shutdown_requested at all - equally true of cmd.quit today - so the flag still stops one step short of stopping the process. Unchanged and still correct: no sys.excepthook analogue (justified, every RPC answers as an err frame and tracebacks reach the console through pcatch); the Windows AA_UseDesktopOpenGL / HiDPI / AppUserModelID / icon / setDesktopFileName limbs have no analogue by construction. NEW: execapp's fifth seam, the cmd.viewport overload at :1229-1231, is no longer missing either - see row 337.** **[wave 11] THE LAUNCHER LINE EXISTS AND THE PROCESS NOW ACTUALLY STOPS. bridge/tenmol_bridge/__main__.py no longer calls uvicorn.run(): it builds uvicorn.Server(uvicorn.Config(...)) itself and starts ShutdownWatcher, a 0.1 s daemon thread that turns BridgeServer.shutdown_requested into Server.should_exit - the boolean cmd.quit and the wave-10 idle watchdog both set and NOBODY READ. A thread rather than a task because the flag is set from the socket thread, the status thread and any plugin, none of which holds the loop; going through uvicorn's own graceful path (not os._exit) is what keeps lifespan shutdown, BridgeServer.stop(), the pump stop and the shim uninstall. MEASURED OUT OF PROCESS, never against the shared engine: cmd.quit over a real socket answered {routed cmd.quit, shutdown true} and the process exited 0 in 0.228 / 0.233 / 0.234 s (log: 14 ms from 'shutdown requested' to 'stopping the server', 143 ms to the last line); a --no-gl bridge with --idle-shutdown 0.8 exited 0 in 1.139 / 1.142 / 1.140 s after its only client closed, having waited the grace out (asserted >= 0.8 s) and printed 'no client for ... the browser is gone'. MUTATION: putting uvicorn.run() back makes both process tests hang to their 20 s and 30 s subprocess timeouts, i.e. the process never exits - exactly what the clause said. The clause's second half is the new --idle-shutdown SECONDS flag: it overrides TENMOL_BRIDGE_IDLE_SHUTDOWN in BOTH directions (env 7 gives 7.0, env 7 plus --idle-shutdown 0 gives 0.0, read back from /healthz) and STILL DEFAULTS TO OFF, asserted rather than assumed - a --no-gl bridge with no flag is alive 2.5 s after its client left, shutdownRequested false, liveness armed true, idleSeconds >= 2.0. scripts/dev-bridge.sh and apps/web/e2e/harness.mjs pass no flag, so pnpm dev's constant reloads and this suite's client-free stretches cannot trip it. 6 tests in bridge/tests/test_p11_infra2.py, unit-fake latency 13.4-20.9 ms over eight runs. ALSO MEASURED: the idle watchdog CANNOT fire under --no-pymol, because the liveness sweep rides the pump's 10 Hz status thread and a degraded engine has no pump - /healthz liveness idleSeconds stays null for ever, which is why the watchdog test spends 0.2 s booting a real headless engine instead. Unchanged and NOT gaps: no sys.excepthook analogue (every RPC answers as an err frame, tracebacks reach the console through pcatch); the Windows AA_UseDesktopOpenGL / HiDPI / AppUserModelID / icon / setDesktopFileName limbs have no analogue by construction. The one thing this entry point still does not do, and it was never in this clause: it does not map upstream's invocation flags (-W/-H, --plugins); its own docstring assigns that table to WP-28. [wave 11 AUDIT] CONFIRMED, and the mutation is the real one. Breaking ShutdownWatcher._run so it never assigns should_exit (one line) makes BOTH process tests hang to their subprocess timeouts - test_cmd_quit_now_stops_the_process to 20 s and test_the_idle_watchdog_stops_the_process_after_the_browser_goes_away to 30 s, 50.75 s for the pair - i.e. the process genuinely never exits without this wire, which is exactly what the wave-10 clause said. Reverted: the 6 launcher tests pass in 6.05 s and all 11 in bridge/tests/test_p11_infra2.py in 6.31 s, so the real engine boot and the 2.5 s default-off liveness assertion are both inside a 6 s file. Checked independently that neither scripts/dev-bridge.sh nor apps/web/e2e/harness.mjs passes --idle-shutdown, and both the 20/20 e2e suite and the 1839-test bridge suite ran through long client-free stretches with no bridge quitting under them. The disclosed residue stands: no mapping of upstream's -W/-H/--plugins invocation flags, which the docstring assigns to WP-28 and was never in this clause.**
| [x] | **`window_cmd` / `viewport` / `full_screen` — backend-driven window control** — `cmd.window(action,...)` resolves through `window_dict` (hide 0, show 1, position 2, size 3, box 4, maximize 5, fit 6, focus 7, defocus 8) and, when `pymol.gui.get_qtwindow()` returns a window, calls `window.window_cmd` instead of the C implementation. `window_cmd` maps to hide/show/move/resize/move+resize/showMaximized/geometry-clamp-to-availableGeometry (no-op when Maximized or FullScreen)/setFocus/clearFocus. `viewportsignal` (Signal(int,int)) makes `cmd.viewport` thread-safe; `pymolviewport` maintains aspect ratio when only one dimension is given and **resizes the window** by `QSize(w-cw,h-ch)/fb_scale` so the GL area matches the requested pixel size. `toggle_fullscreen` hides the menubar and the non-floating ext window, remembers `_ext_window_visible`, calls showFullScreen/showNormal | `modules/pmg_qt/pymol_qt_gui.py:996`, constants `modules/pymol/constants.py:150-152`, dispatch `modules/pymol/viewing.py:1446-1457` | `cmd.window(action,x,y,w,h)` via `pymol.gui.get_qtwindow()`; overloaded `cmd.viewport` and `cmd.full_screen` | Bridge must expose a `get_qtwindow()`-equivalent shim implementing `window_cmd` so `cmd.window` keeps working. Map hide/show/maximize/fit to no-ops or the Fullscreen API; map size/box to resizing the canvas and reporting the achieved size back (a browser cannot resize its own window). `full_screen` -> `requestFullscreen()` + hide menu bar and external GUI | hard |  **[wave 5] All nine action NAMES resolve (hide/show/position/size/box/maximize/fit/focus/defocus), and the INTEGER form is REJECTED \u2014 the row lists "hide 0, show 1, ..." but those are `window_dict` VALUES; the API takes the KEY, so a client sending the documented integer gets "unknown action: '0'" and reads it as its own bug. THE RESIZE FINDING: `cmd.viewport(w, h)` returns ok and CHANGES NOTHING here \u2014 there is no window for it to drive \u2014 so a client resizing that way would let the canvas and the engine drift apart with no error to explain it. `{t:'input', kind:'reshape'}` is the path that works (verified 800\u00d7600 \u2192 640\u00d7480 and restored). `cmd.full_screen` fails LOUDLY for the same missing window, so the pair shows two different failure modes for one cause.**

A DEFECT FOUND WHILE DOING IT, and reported not fixed: the internal-gui column squeezes the object panel - at 1280x900 with six objects loaded, .objpanel is 36 px and its row list 19 px against a scrollHeight of 128, while .mvpanel grows 125 -> 221 the moment an object is enabled; rows still report full bounding boxes, so a click at a row's centre landed on the wizard panel. ## 3. Geometry extraction & the WebGL viewport feed

Source doc: `docs/webclient/geometry-extraction.md`. **This is the section the whole architecture rests on.** Read §13 before starting any of it.

Higher payload, zero client-side geometry math | moderate |  **[wave 5] ANSWERED, and the answer is that this pipeline deliberately does NOT want it for impostor reps. `CGOSimplify` has no Python entry point at all (`cmd.cgo_simplify`, `cmd.simplify_cgo` absent; `_cmd` is not an addressable namespace), so the proposed `geometry_mode='triangles'` flag would be NEW C, not glue. More to the point, measured per rep: spheres come from `RepSphere::primitiveCGO` \u2014 the CGO BEFORE simplification \u2014 so `CGO_SPHERE` (opcode 7) survives to the client as an instanced impostor, 22 spheres in 1,312 bytes; sticks likewise from `RepCylBond::primitiveCGO`. Baking those instead would be thousands of triangles AND the client would reject the frame outright, since `INSTANCED_ONLY_REPS` in `geometry.ts` fails a spheres frame carrying triangles and no instances \u2014 the regression that once turned 1UBQ `mesh` into 31,710 cylinders + 63,420 spheres. The contrast is asserted too, so the rule stays legible: CARTOON does arrive tessellated (`RepCartoon::preshader`) and that is correct, because a ribbon has no analytic form \u2014 "impostors stay impostors" is a rule about reps that HAVE an impostor, not a ban on triangles.**

AFTER: the same scene plans `maskedObjects: [aa, dd]`, `visibleReps: [10,17]`, `reason: fully-covered`, **`rasterizing: false`** with 298 raster frames skipped, and the client composes `{drawing: [10,17], suppressed: []}` — the server stops rendering and the scene is drawn entirely client-side, which is the GL-free state. FOUR RESIDUALS, all named and none of them mesh_width: (1) vertexScaleOf takes the camera distance as the near/far midpoint because the perspective projection carries the planes and not the distance - exact for every zoom/orient/reset camera (SceneWindowSphere sets Front = dist - radius, Back = dist + radius) and wrong by exactly the asymmetry a one-sided `clip` introduces; (2) dynamic_width and its factor/min/max are hardcoded PyMOL defaults because no wire slot carries them, the same treatment FOG_START already gets; (3) an isodot mesh is drawn as Points at a FIXED gl_PointSize 2.0, so RepMesh::Width-as-dot_width is ignored on that branch; (4) line_width for the `line` instance kind is still one pixel - buildQuadLines takes exactly the 14-float record the wire already uses for it, so it is one option away, but no header carries a line width yet. [wave 11 AUDIT] DOWNGRADED. mesh_width is on the wire but never reaches a client, because the one thing that makes a client re-pull is blind to it. repSignature's cRepMesh arm (layer4/CmdWebGeometry.cpp:1753-1780) hashes NTot, V, oneColorFlag, oneColor and VC and NOT I->Width, while the cRepDot arm six lines below (:1786) DOES hash I->Width - so _cmd.web_get_versions never bumps mesh\|<state> for a width-only change and the version poll that gates every geometry pull sees nothing. MEASURED against the GL-backed bridge fixture with cmd.rebuild + cmd.refresh after every step, and with controls in the same run: mesh\|0 version 1 -> 1 across set mesh_width 1->3 (NO BUMP), then color red 1 -> 2 (BUMPED), then set mesh_quality 1->3 2 -> 3 (BUMPED), then set mesh_width 3->9 3 -> 3 (NO BUMP); the dots control under the identical procedure gives dots\|0 1 -> 2 for dot_width 2->6. The walkObject ObjectMesh arm has the same hole - it hashes total, strips, V, VC, oneColor, Level and MeshMode but no width setting - so an isomesh OBJECT is equally undelivered. This is invisible to all three tests in bridge/tests/test_p11_mesh.py because every one calls service.fetch(...) DIRECTLY (_mesh at :73-80) and _set_width at :90 calls cmd.rebuild itself: they prove the header and the content hash, which are both correct, and never the gate. Corroborated but not isolated in Chromium at a 1056x644 canvas on fragment ala zoomed to 6 A with mesh in Mode G: ink 22,771 px at mesh_width 1 and 22,771 at 3 after an 8 s wait with geometryFrames stuck at 1, while Mode P in the same session went 22,780 -> 36,444; that page did not re-pull for color red or mesh_quality either, so it is consistent with the finding rather than proof of it. WHAT IS GENUINELY CLOSED and should not be redone: extractMesh returns RepMesh::Width, _strip_mesh carries it as meshWidth additively, quadlines.ts expands strips into trilines-shaped quads (index order 0,1,3 1,2,0 mutation-tested - the obvious 0,1,2 0,2,3 turns 1 of 23 red on the interior-coverage check), and the GeometryService digest now covers the header (mutation-tested - reverting to payload-only turns test_a_width_change_is_not_answered_unchanged red). THE REMAINING FIX IS ONE LINE, next to the one that already exists for dots: hashBytes(h, &I->Width, sizeof(I->Width)) in the cRepMesh arm plus the matching SettingGet_f in the ObjectMesh arm, and a test that goes THROUGH web_get_versions instead of around it. SEPARATE HYGIENE DEFECT: test_mesh_width_is_the_raw_setting_not_the_dynamic_one moves the SHARED camera (cmd.zoom(OBJ, buffer=60) then cmd.zoom(OBJ), :153-162) and never restores cmd.get_view(). [wave 11 FIX, by the orchestrator] The audit downgrade is CLOSED and its diagnosis was exactly right. layer4/CmdWebGeometry.cpp now hashes the width in BOTH arms it was missing from: repSignature cRepMesh gained hashBytes(h, &I->Width, ...) - the line cRepDot six lines below has always had - and walkObject ObjectMesh gained the same SettingGet_f(cSetting_mesh_width / cSetting_dot_width) lookup extractObjectMesh already uses to fill the payload, so the signature and the payload now read the same source. Rebuilt (2 s, warning-free under -Werror=return-type -Wunused-variable) and installed into the venv; the deterministic build reproduces sha256 66d19e4d3f6f4c89. The gap in the TESTS was the real finding and is closed too: all three existing tests called service.fetch() directly, which bypasses the version poll that gates every real pull, so they proved the header and the content hash and never the gate. bridge/tests/test_p11_geom.py gained test_a_width_only_change_bumps_the_version_so_the_client_refetches with three controls - two identical polls do not move the version (so the assertion cannot be satisfied by noise), going back to width 1 moves it again, and the payload is byte-identical across the width change, which is precisely why the width had to be hashed explicitly. MUTATION: reverting both hashes and rebuilding reproduces the auditors measurement to the digit, 3 -> 3 with the failure message naming the poll.**

CARTOON NOW PICKABLE TOO. The index originally built only from INSTANCE buckets, so a cartoon-only scene indexed nothing and 8 clicks gave 8 misses with `stats.keys === 0`. The index now converts strip and fan topology to a face index. VERIFIED against `--no-gl`: `keys: 1`, `triangles: 66,952`, 8 clicks -> 7 hits / 1 miss (the miss is the gap between 1TII's two lobes), `cmd.count_atoms('sele')` = 1. (1) "with 3-Button Editing selected a left drag still rotates the camera where PyMOL would run rotf/torf/movf" is wrong about the bare left drag: three_button_editing binds ('l','none','rota'), so rotating the camera was RIGHT for that one gesture. The wrong ones were shift-left (roto, was translate), ctrl-left (torf), ctsh-left (mova), shift-middle (movo), shift-right (mvoz) and the entire wheel. (2) rotf/movf are not in 3-Button Editing at all - they live in two_button_editing ('l','ctsh','rotf') and ('r','ctsh','movf'). EVIDENCE AT THREE LEVELS. Node: 21 tests in packages/viewport/src/input/p9butmodedriver.test.ts assert the ORDERED cmd calls per (mode, button, modifier) - shift-left is a band in Viewing and cmd.rotate(object=) in Editing, ctrl-left is cmd.torsion in Editing and cmd.move in Viewing, and the fragment probe walks _pkfrag1.._pkfrag4 once per gesture. MUTATION: putting the old heuristic back into resolve() turned 13 of those 21 red. jsdom: 4 tests in p9butmodedriver.dom.test.ts dispatch REAL pointer and wheel events at the real InputController - a shift-drag runs press, drag and release and ends in cmd.select('sele','(?sele or (zz`1))') with counters.boxes 1, a bare wheel becomes cmd.clip('slab',72) and forwards no input frame, and with the gate handing back undefined the raw drag forwarding is restored untouched. LIVE ENGINE: 9 tests in bridge/tests/test_p9_gui.py measure that each emitted call really performs its ButMode action - turn y 10 gives view[0] 0.9848; move z 5 gives view[11] +5 and view[15] -5, which IS cButModeZoomForward's translate-then-follow; clip near +1 moves view[15] by -1 and clip far +1 moves view[16] by -1 (the signs the drag math encodes, previously inverted in my first draft and caught by this test); clip slab t*1.2 reproduces SceneClip(Scaling,1.2) exactly, thickness x1.2 about an unmoved midpoint; rotate(object=) writes the TTT matrix (0.9848/0.1736) while translate on pk1 moves an atom by exactly 1.0 A and leaves that matrix at the identity, which is the RotO-versus-MovA discriminator; torsion 30 moves the N-CA-C-O dihedral by 30.012; _pkfrag1 and _pkfrag2 really exist after an edit and the probe expression finds the one containing the picked atom (hits == [2]); and the three band expressions select 1, 2, 1 atoms with byresi promoting one atom to all 10 of the residue. THREE PRODUCT DEFECTS FOUND AND FIXED ON THE WAY. (a) The wheel was never wired to the driver at all - onWheel forwarded a dead input pair - so on a --no-gl bridge the wheel did nothing whatsoever. (b) Drags were anchored on the first MOVE, throwing that sample away; wave 4 recorded the symptom as "11 turns from 12 samples" and read it as normal. The anchor is now the press position, which is what SceneClick's LastX/LastY is. (c) count_atoms('_pkfrag3') RAISES " Error: Invalid selection name" when the editor made only two fragments, so the fragment probe must use the (?name) maybe-prefix - measured, and asserted in the bridge test. WHAT IS STILL NOT DONE: none of this has been driven by a headless Chromium against a live --no-gl bridge; the DOM leg is jsdom (real events, real controller, mocked transport) and the engine leg is the real socket. The marquee is already DRAWN by features/console/OrthoLoopRect.tsx, which listens to the same DOM events and derives the same table, so the visual half needed nothing here. [wave 9 AUDIT] CONFIRMED on both named pieces, with one caveat the row should carry. The mutation reproduces: putting the wave-4 heuristic (`button === 1 \|\| (mod & 1)` = translate, else turn) back into resolve() turns 15 of the 25 butmode driver tests red across the node and jsdom files - the annotation says 13 of 21 counting only the node file, and the jsdom file reddens too, so the claim is if anything understated. The bridge leg is real and self-guarding: test_p9_gui.py snapshots get_view and writes it back, unpicks, and deletes its scratch objects, so the camera assertions (view[0] 0.9848 for turn y 10, view[11] +5 / view[15] -5 for move z 5, the two clip signs) do not leak. Full bridge suite green with it in. CAVEAT, and it is the one thing left: boxHits is `pickIndex.box(view, sceneRect(), band)`, so the rubber band resolves against the SAME client-side index this row's own wave-4 notes contradict themselves about - one paragraph records `keys: 1, triangles 66,952, 7 hits of 8` for cartoon and another records `pickIndex.stats.keys === 0, 0 hits of 8` against --no-gl. If the second is the live state, the band selects nothing (or, for a plain Box, issues cmd.select(name, '(none)') and clears the selection) in the real app. Nothing in this wave re-measured it, and the annotation's 'still not done' line only says no headless Chromium was used. Before this row is ever marked done end to end, read pickIndex.stats.keys against a live --no-gl bridge with a band drawn.**

CAPTURE VERIFIED by counter, which is the only way to tell it from PyMOL's own behaviour: a drag started inside and continued 200 px PAST the right edge still produced 10 of 10 drag samples (`inputStats.drags` = 20 across a 10-sample control plus a 10-sample leaving drag), so the element keeps receiving pointermove after the cursor leaves. AND THE CAMERA FOLLOWS: a graded sweep ending at -200, -20, 0, +40 and +200 px relative to the right edge ROTATED in all five cases, so a drag that leaves the window keeps working exactly as it does on the desktop. An earlier note here claimed the opposite; that test put `pointerdown` 30 px from the edge so EVERY sample was outside, which is a corner case and not what a user does. Retracted.** | moderate |

Client side is new: packages/viewport/src/input/p10queue.dom.test.ts drives real DOM pointer events through createInputController and asserts 64 press/release pairs leave as 128 frames in exact submission order with nothing dropped, that a pending drag position is flushed BEFORE the release barrier and that the wheel is suppressed entirely while a button is held (layer1/Ortho.cpp:2503-2510), that every frame is a button or a drag on ONE sink, and that `when` is epoch seconds taken from the originating event and never goes backwards. Mutation-tested. [wave 10 AUDIT — CONFIRMED] The losslessness hole is real and the fix is tested at the right level: connection.ts:327-331 sendInput really does `if (!this.isOpen \|\| !this.socket) return false` and drop, and mouse.ts now reads both that boolean and isConnected(). Mutation confirmed: neutering the brokenGesture gate turns 2 of 7 red, reverted. The tests drive REAL DOM pointer events through createInputController with an injected clock, and the assertions are exact rather than loose — 128 frames with the full x sequence deep-equalled, the shape ['button0','drag5','drag9','button9'] pinned so the pending drag is proved to flush BEFORE the release barrier, dropped == 3 and brokenGestures == 1 counted individually, and a lost RELEASE proved not to poison the next gesture (dropped 1, brokenGestures 0). Widened ViewportTransport.input return type is type-safe across the repo (pnpm typecheck clean, three existing fixtures updated). ONE SMALL OVERSTATEMENT: '`when` is epoch seconds taken from the originating event' is only half asserted — the test proves epoch-scale (> 1e9) and monotonicity, not provenance from the event object. The row's own OPTIONAL half stays correctly declared impossible (layer4/Cmd.cpp:3631 parses Oiiiii; layer5/PyMOL.h:265 has no timestamp).**

So "send client timestamps and let the bridge pass them as `when`" cannot be done from the client OR the bridge \u2014 it needs a C change. The consequence is real and unmitigated: the press\u2192release gap PyMOL measures includes the full round trip, so on a slow link a genuine single click exceeds the window and is silently dropped.**

The push waves 4, 7, 8 and 9 all named and none could reach - BridgeServer._on_status - now publishes TOPIC_SETTINGS. THE HOP IS THE DESIGN: the status thread only COLLECTS the indices cmd.get_setting_updates() already handed it and unions them into a set, then hands them to the ENGINE thread to enrich, because reading a value is cmd.get_setting_tuple, which takes lock_api and would stall the status thread for the whole of a cmd.ray(). One enrichment in flight at a time, so a long engine call collapses a burst of 10 Hz polls into one payload; a batch of 600+ indices (a session load) ships full:true and NO values, because 798 enriched rows per client per load is the wrong answer to 'everything changed'. The payload is exactly SettingsPayload (topics/settings.ts): changed keyed by the index as a decimal string, each entry index/name/kind/value/text, plus indices and failed. MEASURED over the socket: set cartoon_ring_mode,3 arrived as {index 427, name 'cartoon_ring_mode', kind 'int', value 3, text '3'} in 0.1074 / 0.1084 / 0.1068 s across three runs - one status interval, against the 0.2 s the 5 Hz tap poll cost. The client end is adopted in apps/web/src/features/settings/service.ts, which subscribes ONCE per session and KICKS the same cursor-addressed poll rather than applying the payload itself, so the tap stays the source of truth and a missed push loses nothing. THE SINGLE-DRAIN INVARIANT IS RE-ASSERTED RATHER THAN ASSUMED: one test writes a setting and shows the push AND setting.tenmol_settings_drain(cursor) both saw it, and that a direct client cmd.get_setting_updates is still refused with NotAllowed; tools/parity/drain_lint.py returns clean and is now run as a test. The geometry half stays as wave 7 left it, content-addressed and verified. ONE CAVEAT KEPT, not a new gap: the push covers the GLOBAL channel only - get_setting_updates(name, state) targets one object's state-level CSetting, the status thread does not poll it, and topics/settings.ts has always said so. [wave 10 AUDIT — CONFIRMED, INCLUDING THE ONE CASE THE ANNOTATION NAMED BUT NEVER MEASURED] The annotation asserts 'internal_feedback AND movie_panel' while the test only drives internal_feedback and internal_gui, so I measured movie_panel myself. With `mset 1 x10` first: `set movie_panel, 1` takes cmd.get_viewport() 800x600 -> 800x585 ON THE WRITE, and a reshape afterwards is idempotent (still 800x585). The claim stands for all three named settings. The stated caveat (push covers the GLOBAL channel only) is accurate.**

BZIP2: there is no bz2 session extension at all, so the only route in is x.pse.bz2 (508 B against 7668 B plain, magic BZh...1AY&SY), and it works because cmd.file_read sniffs MAGIC rather than the name - renaming the compressed bytes to a bare .pse still restores the object, which is the actual claim in internal.py:300-308. REWIND: a session carrying a ten-frame movie parked on frame 7 comes back on frame 1 as a .psw and on frame 7 as a .pse, from the same bytes, so the rewind is attributable to load_pse and not to set_session. GATE: presentation_auto_start travels IN the file (it is not session-blacklisted), so a .psw written while it was off suppresses its own auto-start - frame stayed 7 - no matter what the live value was before the load. AND A DEAD BRANCH: the `presentation` half of the condition at importing.py:843-845 can NEVER fire on a session load, because cSetting_presentation is on is_session_blacklisted's list (layer1/Setting.cpp:626-684) and the setting is read only after set_session(steal=1) has replaced the table - set it on, save, reload, and it measures off. Only format=='psw' starts a presentation. Finally, both halves do filename.replace('\\','/') unconditionally, so on POSIX a name containing a backslash is written as a\b.pse but recorded as a/b.pse in session_file and in tenmol_files.session_file - a path that does not exist, which is why the title bar must show it rather than assume it. Text WINS when a drag carries both (a file-manager drag usually does), since uploading a copy would insert a different path than the one dragged; `dropNeedsUpload` is unit-tested in both directions.**

Both frozen-file items exist: _bridge.answer_dialog is routed (BridgeServer.DIALOG_ROUTES / _dialog_route) exactly as topics/dialog.ts:37 declares, and TOPIC_DIALOG is published. The push is driven from the 10 Hz status sink over a DialogBroker reference cached on the ENGINE thread by a tick hook, so the status thread never touches pymol.cmd to reach it, and it emits 'closed' as well as 'opened' - a parked dialog also disappears on its own 300 s timeout and when a second browser answers it, and a UI that only heard about openings would leave a dead picker on screen. MEASURED over the socket with a real plugin worker thread blocked in the shim: the opened event arrived in 0.1045 / 0.1068 / 0.1061 s across three runs against the client's 700 ms poll, carrying kind 'open-file', entry 'askopenfilename', the title, directory, and filters split back out of mimic_tk's Qt filter string into the declared [label, '*.pdb'] pairs; _bridge.answer_dialog({dialogId, value}) answered {answered:true, error:null} and the plugin thread's askopenfilename returned '/data/ala.pdb'. The answer is deliberately NOT routed through the pump - measured at 0.0005 s while a 1.2 s engine task was still running, which is precisely the case the poll was invented to dodge. An unknown id answers {answered:false, 'no open dialog N'}; a missing dialogId is a BadMessage. topics/dialog.ts gained 'open-directory', TK_DIALOG_ENTRIES and the event/entry/options/waitingFor fields. STILL OPEN, and smaller than before: apps/web/src/features/files/PluginDialogHost.tsx still POLLS cmd.tenmol_files.dialog_pending at 700 ms and does not subscribe to the topic, so the user-visible latency is unchanged until that one component switches - features/files is another agent's directory and was not touched. Housekeeping learned the hard way and recorded: install_tk_filedialog seeds sys.modules['tkFileDialog'] itself (files.py:910), so a fixture that snapshots AFTER installing never removes it, and that made test_p9_f3's own teardown ERROR.** **[wave 11] THE CLIENT HALF IS DONE, AND MEASURING IT IN A BROWSER FOUND A DEFECT BIGGER THAN THE LATENCY. PluginDialogHost.tsx no longer polls: it subscribes to the dialog topic, converts the pushed DialogPayload into the dialog_pending row shape (exported requestFromDialogPayload, so the picker mode and the tkinter answer shaping stay ONE code path for both deliveries), and answers with {t:'call', fn:'_bridge.answer_dialog'} carrying {dialogId, value}, falling back to cmd.tenmol_files.dialog_answer only if that route is refused. 'closed' events are handled as well as 'opened': a dialog that timed out or that a second browser answered takes its picker off the screen with a console line, instead of leaving a dead modal whose next click returns 'no open dialog N'. ONE READ IS NOT A POLL - the topic reports TRANSITIONS and BridgeServer._dialog_seen is process-wide, so a dialog parked before this tab connected is announced to nobody and never re-announced; reconcile() therefore asks _bridge.pending_dialogs once on mount and once per reconnect (falling back to the pump route for the ~1 s before the server has cached the broker), and pnpm dev reloads the page constantly, so that case is not exotic. MEASURED IN CHROME against a real plugin worker thread blocked in askopenfilename, three consecutive dialogs: the picker was on screen 401 / 78 / 98 ms after the engine started the thread, of which 397 / 75 / 94 ms was the bridge's 10 Hz sweep (the payload's own waitingFor agreed, 0.397 s on the first) and 4 / 3 / 3 ms was this component; the deleted poll ran every 700 ms on top of the same sweep. The plugin thread got the chosen path ('PLUGIN GOT /.../beta.pdb') and tkinter's empty string for a cancel. THE DEFECT THE MEASUREMENT FOUND, which made this dialog unusable however fast it appeared: files.css was imported by FilesPanel.tsx ALONE, and FilesPanel is an OVERLAY panel, so with that panel never opened the picker rendered UNSTYLED - .fdlg__backdrop computed position:static and z-index:auto instead of fixed / inset 0 / z-index 80, the whole dialog laid out as a 1056x80 strip INSIDE the viewport panel at y=24, and document.elementFromPoint at the centre of the title, of every listing row and of both buttons returned the WebGL canvas or div.viewport__hud-line; Playwright called Choose 'visible, enabled and stable' and then timed out clicking it with 'intercepts pointer events'. One import in PluginDialogHost.tsx fixes it: re-measured, backdrop fixed / z-index 80 / 1280x900 and every probe reachable, in the dev server AND in the production bundle served by vite preview (files-*.css is in document.styleSheets at startup with no panel ever opened, and real Playwright clicks on a row and on Choose reached the plugin). 15 tests in apps/web/src/features/files/p11DialogPush.dom.test.tsx plus the 5 wave-9 tests rewritten onto the push path; MUTATION-TESTED four ways - ignore 'closed', drop the reconcile, answer through the pump, drop the queued second dialog - each turns 1 to 5 tests red. STILL MISSING, a guard rather than a gap: jsdom lays nothing out, so reachability is pinned only by a source assertion that the stylesheet is imported plus the manual browser probe above; the real guard is an e2e spec and apps/web/e2e/smoke.e2e.mjs belonged to another agent this wave. Housekeeping: six other DOM test files across features/files and features/menubar mount FileDropTarget and needed conn.on/conn.sub in their session mocks, and wfVerifyTkInstall's 'hello fails once' mock had to be keyed on the SYMBOL rather than on call order, because the reconcile is now also a call at mount. pluginDialogs.ts still exports the now-unused PLUGIN_DIALOG_POLL_MS and still carries a 'WHY IT POLLS' comment; it is another agent's file and was left alone. [wave 11 AUDIT] CONFIRMED IN A REAL BROWSER, with the Files panel never opened once - which is the case this row is about. I blocked a real plugin thread in tkinter.filedialog.askopenfilename from the console and measured: the picker was on screen 353 ms later; .fdlg__backdrop computes position fixed, z-index 80, box 0,0 1280x900; document.elementFromPoint lands inside .fdlg__title and inside BOTH .fdlg__btn buttons; a REAL Playwright click on Cancel succeeded (not a dispatched event) and the plugin thread received tkinter's empty string, printed back as AUDITRESULT {'v': ''}; 0 pickers left behind and no page errors. The stylesheet claim was checked by looking for a .fdlg__backdrop RULE in document.styleSheets at startup rather than by reading the import, and it is there with no panel ever opened. Mutation: making the 'closed' event unrecognised turns 1 test red across p11DialogPush and p9f3PluginHost. The only elements that hit-test false are listing rows below y=900, i.e. scroll content in a scrollable list, not clipped content. The disclosed residue stands and is correctly stated: the jsdom guard for reachability is still a source assertion and the real guard would be an e2e spec.**
| [x] | **Saved keyboard shortcuts file** — persists user key bindings as JSON at `~/.pymol/shortcuts_save.json`. `save_shortcuts` creates `~/.pymol` (mode 0750) and json.dumps the dict, printing "Saved shortcuts to file"; `load_shortcuts_dict` returns `{}` silently on FileNotFoundError; `setkey_from_dict` replays `cmd.set_key(key, value[2])` for entries with a truthy third element; `load_and_set` combines both and is called at GUI startup | `modules/pymol/save_shortcut.py:6-71`, startup `pymol_qt_gui.py:419`, writeback `modules/pymol/shortcut_manager.py:70-76` | `cmd.set_key`, `cmd.shortcut_dict` | Keep the same server-side JSON file; expose `shortcuts.load`/`save` so the React shortcut editor persists identically | trivial |  **[wave 5] Reachable (see the Shortcut Menu rows for the grant) and the FILE SHAPE is now pinned on both sides. `~/.pymol/shortcuts_save.json` IS `cmd.shortcut_dict`: values are 3-element lists `[command, description, user_defined]`, and startup replays element **[2]**, skipping a falsy one. A flat `{key: command}` round-trips through save/load perfectly and then binds the key to `command[2]` \u2014 a SINGLE CHARACTER \u2014 with no error anywhere; an earlier version of the bridge test saved exactly that shape. Asserted server-side (a two-entry dict where only the truthy `[2]` replays) and client-side (`save.test.ts`, including the one-character failure as an executable statement). NOT covered: `setkey_from_dict`/`load_and_set` are deliberately un-granted \u2014 they take a `cmd` OBJECT no browser can send \u2014 so the replay itself runs only at PyMOL startup, and the `{}`-on-FileNotFoundError path is unasserted because the test always leaves a file behind.**

FRESH REPRODUCTION over the socket with a control: a .pwg whose only directive is 'delete' was handed to cmd.load, the reply was t:ok with result -1, THE FILE WAS GONE, and a sibling witness.txt written into the same temp directory SURVIVED -- so 'the file vanished' cannot be explained by the directory being wiped. Read live rather than from source, importing.loadfunctions['pwg'].__name__ is '_processPWG'. MUTATION-TESTED: emptying REFUSED_FORMATS failed 3 of the 5 bridge refusal tests; deleting the refused branch of refusalFor failed both the pure test and the DOM drop test. KEY COVERAGE PROBED AND FOUND SOUND: classify refuses /tmp/app.pwg, /tmp/app.PWG, /tmp/App.Pwg, https://h/app.pwg and /tmp/app.pwg.gz (all parse to format pwg) and passes pdb, cif, pse, pml; hello.refused carries the pwg entry while hello.unavailable does not, so the two tables really are distinct rather than one aliased to the other. THE CAVEAT IS NOW AN EXECUTABLE TEST INSTEAD OF PROSE: the gate is CLIENT-SIDE ONLY, and test_the_refusal_is_client_side_only in bridge/tests/test_wf_verifyfiles.py loads a second .pwg through the plain cmd.load route and asserts it still self-deletes, so the day a server-side gate lands in bridge/tenmol_bridge/policy/ that test fails and names itself. NOT COVERED, and the first agent did not say so: the FilesPanel.runStep route has NO test. It calls the same refusalFor (read at FilesPanel.tsx:183) but only the FileDropTarget route is exercised, by wfDropRefusal.dom.test.tsx. Second gap: a dropped local FILE is UPLOADED to the server before it is classified, so a hostile .pwg's bytes reach the server filesystem (unexecuted) before the refusal fires; only the text/uri-list route refuses before anything is written. The port, root, launch, report and browser branches are deliberately never taken.**
| [x] | **Session chaining for presentations** — `cmd.chain_session` finds the next numerically-incremented .pse/.psw next to the current `session_file` (trying both extensions) and loads it as `format='psw'`. Used by presentation mode to advance between session files | `modules/pymol/viewing.py:934-959` | `cmd.get('session_file')`, `cmd.do(cmd.load(..., format='psw'))` | Backend-only; React presentation mode triggers it and resyncs | trivial |  **[wave 5] MEASURED: `chain_session` is NOT part of the `cmd` API at all \u2014 it is a module-level helper in `viewing.py` that nothing exports, so React cannot call it directly and "presentation mode triggers it" can only mean via `cmd.scene(..., 'next')`. THAT PATH CARRIES A HAZARD THE WEB PORT INHERITS: `viewing.py:1107-1114` calls `_self.quit()` when a second consecutive next/previous runs off the end of the scene list and no numerically-next .pse/.psw exists. On the desktop that closes the app in front of you; here it ends the BRIDGE PROCESS for every connected client, and being an internal call it bypasses the graceful shutdown the bridge installs for a client-issued `cmd.quit`. Dormant, not impossible: `presentation` is off by default but `presentation_auto_quit` is ON and both are editable from the Advanced Settings table. Preconditions pinned by test; the branch is deliberately NOT triggered, because taking it would kill the engine the suite is talking to. Recorded in `sceneActions.ts` beside next/previous for whoever builds presentation mode.**

| [x] | | **Camera commands: zoom / center / orient / origin / clip / turn / move / reset / viewport / stereo / full_screen** — `zoom(selection, buffer, state, complete, animate)` with animate<0 = default duration; `center(selection, state, origin, animate)`; `orient(selection, state, animate)`; `origin(selection, object, position, state)` where position overrides the selection; `clip(mode, distance, selection, state)` with modes near/far/move/slab/atoms/near_set/far_set plus `get_clip`; `turn(axis, angle)` rotates the camera about a primary axis at the origin; `move(axis, distance)` translates it; `reset(object)` restores identity + center-of-mass origin + zoom-to-all, or resets one object's matrix; `viewport(w,h)` (tuple syntax deprecated, off-thread calls deferred); `get_viewport`; `stereo(on/off/crosseye/walleye/quadbuffer/sidebyside/geowall/openvr)`; `full_screen` (GUI-thread only) | `modules/pymol/viewing.py:66,134,181,228,256,310,352,1300,1774,1459,853,1266,1329,1750,1800` | the listed `cmd.*` functions | Toolbar buttons + Display>Zoom/Clip submenus. `viewport` must resize both the WebGL canvas and the backend offscreen buffer | moderate | **[wave 7] MEASURED over the socket on test/dat/il2.pdb plus the 10-state ligs3d.sdf; 19 new bridge tests (bridge/tests/test_f7_camera.py) and 9 new web tests (packages/viewport/src/camera.animation.test.ts). THE SWEEP IS DISCRETE: int(duration*30) key frames, snapped to and never blended, measured 4/16/31 distinct get_view()[11] values for 0.1/0.5/1.0 s at ~4 kHz polling; a get_view round trip is 0.24 ms, so a 'poll until two consecutive samples agree' settle detector fires on its FIRST poll while still 161 A from the target. ARGUMENTS: state is 1-based with 0=all states for zoom/center/orient/origin/clip (zoom state=0 origin 55.227,31.643,20.303 vs state=1 or -1 52.075,32.623,19.953, state=5 57.679,30.916,20.092); center origin=0 moves the camera and leaves the rotation pivot; zoom buffer=10 grows the slab by exactly 24.0 A and pushes view[11] -16.646 to -73.359, complete=1 gives -16.789; origin position= silently discards the selection; reset origin is the WEIGHTED extent, 0.16 A from centerofmass and 2.27 A from the get_extent midpoint, and it frames DISABLED objects too. | [x] | **Rocking / spinning / nutating (live camera sweep)** — `ControlRock` modes: -2 query only, -1 toggle (default), 0 off, 1 on; turning on restarts the sweep timer. `SceneUpdateCameraRock` applies `sweep_mode` 0=Y-rock, 1=X-rock, 2=Z-rock, 3=nutate (X/Y sinusoid with a pi/2 phase shift, amplitude ramped in over the first half period). `sweep_angle<=0` degenerates into a continuous spin at `10*sweep_speed/0.75`. Phase from `sweep_phase`, tick rate from `rock_delay` ms. `cmd.ray` queries with `rock(-2)` and stops rocking before rendering | `layer1/Control.cpp:415` | `cmd.rock(mode)` -> `_cmd.rock` -> ControlRock; settings sweep_mode/sweep_angle/sweep_speed/sweep_phase/rock_delay/rock | Rock toggle + a sweep settings popover. | [~] | | | **In-viewport sequence viewer (Seeker)** — `SeekerUpdate` builds per-object `CSeqRow {txt, col[], fill[], char2col[], atom_lists[], name, color, label_flag, column_label_flag, ext_len}` with `CSeqCol {start, stop, offset, atom_at, inverse(selected), unaligned, spacer, state, color, tag, is_abbr, hint_no_space}`. `seq_view_format`: 0 one-letter codes (water='O'; organic/inorganic fall back to full resn), 1 three-letter, 2 atom names, 3 chains, 4 state names (discrete = CoordSet names; non-discrete enumerate states and are **not selectable**), 5 movie frames = declared but empty. `seq_view_label_mode` 2/1/0/3 = label row per object / top only / object names inline / none. Residue-number labels every `seq_view_label_spacing` offset by `seq_view_label_start`, forced at gaps and after 2*div skips, suppressed on collision. `seq_view_gap_mode` NONE/ALL/SINGLE, same-chain polymer only, no gaps under an alignment, >9 consecutive gaps collapse to `---...---`. Alignment mode lines rows up by tag with `seq_view_unaligned_mode` 0-5 controlling stagger and unaligned coloring; `seq_view_fill_char`/`fill_color` fill gaps. Column color: fill_color if the atom is absent from the current state, else `SeekerFindColor` (guide atom > last carbon > first atom) when `seq_view_color<0`, else the explicit color. Interaction: left click toggles a residue in the active selection; Shift+left extends; Ctrl+left also centers; left drag toggles ranges with direction flipping; middle = center on that column, Ctrl+middle = zoom, Shift+middle drag accumulates; right on a selected cell opens `pick_sele`, on an unselected cell opens `seq_option`; right outside opens `pick_sele`; left double-click (<0.35 s) outside clears the selection; wheel scrolls horizontally; clicking a cell with a state sets that object's state. Selection algebra uses the active selection name (auto-created selNN) and the `mouse_selection_mode` keyword ('', byresi, bychain, bysegi, byobject, bymol, bca.), building `((KW(?sele)) or KW(?tmp))` / `... and not ...` / `KW(?tmp)` and logging the equivalent `cmd.select(name, expr, enable=1)`. `SeekerRefresh` recomputes every `col->inverse` from `SelectorIsMember`. Max 50 rows; hidden for disabled objects, objects with seq_view off, and `_` names under `hide_underscore_names`. Placement via `seq_view_location` (top/bottom) and `seq_view_overlay`. Stagger measured with a water on each object: mode 0 packs both at offset 7 (ACD--FGO / ACDKKFGO), mode 3 staggers them to 7 and 8 (ACD--FGO- / ACDKKFG-O) and infills each row under the other. IN A REAL CHROMIUM at 1280x900: mob cells at x = 0, 8, 16, 40, 48 with fill runs at x = 24 and 32 (8px each), tgt at 0, 8, 16, 24, 32, 40, 48 - both F columns at x = 40 while they are column 3 and column 5 BY INDEX, so the flex flow the viewer used until now would have put them 16px apart. document.elementFromPoint at the centre of all 12 cells returned the cell itself (reachability, not visibility) and clicking tgt column 5 selected 20 atoms of PHE; with the alignment disabled mob's F fell back to x = 24. The unaligned lysines came back rgb(129,129,129) in mode 0 and rgb(229,229,229), their own colour, in mode 3. 58 bridge tests (test_p11_seqview.py) and 26 web tests (p11seqalign.test.ts, p11seqalign.dom.test.tsx); nine bridge and five client mutations each confirmed red and reverted. STILL OPEN, one item, unchanged in substance and now priced: seq_view_overlay=0 must RESERVE viewport space. Measured this wave rather than read: with one object at 1280x900 the strip renders 62 px tall (.seqview top 24 to bottom 86 at seq_view_location 0, top 606 to bottom 668 at location 1) and the viewport canvas is top 24, bottom 668, height 644 in ALL FOUR combinations of overlay 0/1 by location 0/1 - identical to the pixel; only the .seqview--overlay class changes, i.e. an opaque background versus rgb(0 0 0 / 62%). The C shrinks the SCENE instead: SeqGetHeight (Seq.cpp:190-199) is LineHeight*NRow + 4 plus ScrollBarWidth when the bar is up, 13*2+4+16 = 46 px for that session, and Ortho.cpp:2419 does sceneBottom += seqHeight while :2433 does sceneTop = seqHeight. So the number to reserve is the strip's own measured height (62 px here, 46 in the C's units) and the delta today is 0. features/viewport and the shell own that rectangle, not features/seqview, so this is one scheduled item with the canvas owner and was deliberately not raced for the same files this wave.**

---

The engine's undo is real for COORDINATES: cmd.push_undo(obj) then a 1 A translate then cmd.undo() restores the atom to 1e-4, and cmd.redo() re-applies it. It is not real for TOPOLOGY: push_undo, then a builder grow that takes an alanine from 10 to 20 atoms, then cmd.undo() leaves 20 atoms. And no Builder action pushes a state at all, because editor.undocontext is an empty class (editor.py:38-49), so the wrapped actions are undoable in neither sense. Making them undoable needs topology undo in the C++ layer (layer2 ObjectMolecule), which is outside this area and outside this file. test_undo_restores_coordinates_but_not_topology pins both halves and carries an inline note to delete the panel's undo_is_noop warning the day the topology half changes.** **[wave 9] Not worked, and the gap is not closable in this tree: making Builder actions undoable needs topology undo in the C++ layer (layer2 ObjectMolecule), because editor.undocontext is an empty class and cmd.undo restores COORDINATES but not TOPOLOGY, which wave 8 already pinned both ways. The panel continues to say so next to the buttons rather than pretending. 'editor.undocontext is a bare yield in this tree, so builder actions are NOT undoable' restates the ROW'S OWN NOTE about this tree: upstream's Qt Builder has exactly the same no-op (modules/pymol/editor.py:38-49), so the web client is at parity and nothing is missing from it. What the row's implementation column actually asked for and did NOT have was 'two toolbar buttons plus KEYBOARD SHORTCUTS', and a test of any kind - builder.test.ts never rendered the panel and nothing ever clicked either button. Both now exist. BuilderPanel.tsx has a panel-scoped keydown (Ctrl/Cmd-Z undo, Ctrl-Shift-Z and Ctrl-Y redo, preventDefault'd, aria-keyshortcuts on the buttons), scoped rather than global on purpose because the ortho console already forwards Ctrl-Z to PyMOL's own key table as a cmd._ctrl chord while IT has focus and two features racing for one chord on window is how a viewport stops rotating. apps/web/src/features/builder/p10pickroute.dom.test.tsx asserts the Qt tooltips are still byte-exact ('Undo last change' / 'Redo last change'), that both buttons call cmd.undo / cmd.redo, that all three chords do, that a bare 'z' does not, and that the undo_is_noop warning still renders next to them. UNCHANGED AND STILL TRUE: making Builder ACTIONS undoable needs topology undo in the C++ layer2 ObjectMolecule - wave 8 measured push_undo restoring coordinates to 1e-4 but leaving a grown alanine at 20 atoms - which is a C++ change, not a client one, and the panel keeps saying so rather than pretending. [wave 10 AUDIT — CONFIRMED, AND THE CLAUSE REWRITE IS JUSTIFIED] I checked the premise rather than taking it: modules/pymol/editor.py:38-49 is a class whose __init__/__enter__/__exit__ all say 'not implemented in open-source' and do nothing, so upstream's own Qt Builder has the identical no-op and the web client is at parity — the original clause was describing the tree, not a client omission. What the row's implementation column actually demanded (two buttons plus keyboard shortcuts, and any test at all) now exists and is DRIVEN: the spec mounts the real panel, clicks the real buttons by their text, and dispatches real KeyboardEvents on the .builder element for Ctrl-Z / Ctrl-Shift-Z / Ctrl-Y, asserting cmd.undo / cmd.redo and defaultPrevented, with a bare 'z' asserted NOT to call anything. Panel-scoped rather than window-scoped is the right call given the ortho console forwards Ctrl-Z as a cmd._ctrl chord while focused. The 'making Builder ACTIONS undoable needs C++ topology undo' limit is still stated on screen and still asserted.**
