But the CGO 'stream' cannot exist as geometry: MEASURED and source-confirmed, the VDW bumps are CoordSet::SculptCGO, drawn immediately in the antialias pass (layer2/CoordSet.cpp:1390-1393, built at layer2/Sculpt.cpp:1715-1740), NOT the cRepCGO rep, so _cmd.web_get_rep_geometry has nothing to serve and they exist only in the rasterised pixel stream. Also measured: cmd.show('cgo', obj) is a no-op for a molecule because an ObjectMolecule's visRep already carries bit 13 (cmd.get_vis()[obj][2] contains 13 before and after). REMAINING GAP: the bridge still does not push sculpt deltas on a timer; a client that wants continuous sculpting must drive cmd.sculpt_iterate itself and watch the pixel stream for the bumps.** **[wave 9] The remaining gap is closed, and the fix is that the CLIENT is the idle loop. Sculpting upstream is not a command, it is something the process does while idle: PyMOL_Idle calls ExecutiveSculptIterateAll(G) whenever ControlIdling(G) is true (PyMOL.cpp:2424), and ControlIdling is true exactly when the sculpting setting is on (Control.cpp:397-403). The bridge pump DRAWS and never idles, so on this backend sculpting 1 moved no atom at all - MEASURED, the coordinates are byte-identical after the flag goes on and only move on the first tick. New cmd.builder_sculpt_tick (bridge/tenmol_bridge/panels/builder.py) is one turn of that loop with the same gate, the same object set and the same cycle count: cmd.sculpt_iterate('all', -1, sculpting_cycles), which is the public form of the same walk (Executive.cpp:7164-7173) and returns the TOTAL STRAIN. features/builder/sculptTicker.ts drives it at 20 Hz while the flag is on, with one tick in flight at a time, stopping on its own when a reply says active is false (a set sculpting, 0 typed in the console) or when a tick errors, and the panel shows the strain converging. The moved atoms reach the user on the streams that already exist - the pixel stream immediately, the content-addressed geometry diff on the next 4 Hz scan (row 209) - so no new topic and no bridge-side timer iterating for a client that has gone away. MEASURED on a displaced alanine: strain 18.935, 13.109, 10.719, 9.508, 8.390, 7.368, 6.527 over seven ticks, 10 cycles each, one object. ALSO PINNED: sculpt_iterate on a deactivated object is a byte-exact no-op, which is what lets the ticker stop on Done and be sure nothing else is moving. ONE DIFFERENCE, reported in the reply as auto_center_unsupported rather than hidden: the idle loop passes a centre accumulator when sculpt_auto_center is on and cmd.sculpt_iterate passes nullptr, so the scene does not follow the atoms. Wave 8's other finding stands: the sculpt_vdw_vis_mode bumps are CoordSet::SculptCGO and exist only in the rasterised pixels. [wave 9 AUDIT] DOWNGRADED: the annotation's central premise is REFUTED by measurement. It claims 'the bridge pump DRAWS and never idles, so on this backend sculpting 1 moved no atom at all - MEASURED, the coordinates are byte-identical after the flag goes on and only move on the first tick'. The pump DOES idle: bridge/tenmol_bridge/engine.py:236 is `did_work = bool(self.p.idle())` inside tick(), i.e. PyMOL_Idle at 60 Hz, and PyMOL_Idle is exactly where ExecutiveSculptIterateAll lives (the annotation quotes that C itself). AUDIT MEASURED on a displaced alanine (fragment ala, sculpt_activate, CB x+1.0, rebuild, read coords): set sculpting 1 and then SLEEP 3 s with no client tick and no pixel subscriber gives a max coordinate delta of 0.6812 A; with a pixels subscription, 0.6843 A over 2 s. cmd.refresh alone moves nothing (0.0 after one and after six refreshes), so the mover is the idle loop and not a draw. The bridge test's own `_max_delta(before, after) == 0.0` passes only because it reads the coordinates in the same millisecond as the `set`, before the pump has ticked once - it does not show what the annotation says it shows. TWO CONSEQUENCES. (1) cmd.builder_sculpt_tick and the 20 Hz sculptTicker are not 'the idle loop the bridge does not run': they run a SECOND minimiser beside the engine's own, so a sculpting client iterates at roughly twice sculpting_cycles per frame and the strain the panel shows is not the strain the engine is converging on alone. (2) The row's actual clause - the bridge does not push sculpt deltas on a timer - is answered by nothing measured: no test shows a sculpt coordinate delta arriving on the 4 Hz geometry stream. WHAT STANDS: builder_sculpt_tick exists, is wired into BuilderPanel via sculptTicker.ts, reports auto_center_unsupported, and is non-vacuous (replacing cmd.sculpt_iterate with strain = 0.0 reddens its test). NEW GAP: prove whether the pump's own idle sculpting is enough (in which case the ticker is redundant and should be dropped or gated), and show one sculpt delta reaching the client on the geometry stream.** **[wave 10] BOTH OF THE AUDITOR'S QUESTIONS ANSWERED, one by refutation and one by measurement. (1) The engine's own idle loop sculpts, so the wave-9 premise was wrong and the auditor right: on a displaced alanine with no client tick, no pixel subscriber and no draw request, `set sculpting, 1` then reading in the SAME millisecond gives 0.0000 A (all the wave-9 test ever showed), 2.0 s later gives 0.6843 A, and 1.0 s after `set sculpting, 0` gives 0.0000 A - engine.py:236's self.p.idle() is PyMOL_Idle and ControlIdling is the gate. (2) A SCULPT DELTA DOES REACH THE CLIENT ON A TIMER, which is the row's actual clause and was unproven: with only the idle loop running for 1.5 s, GeometryService.scan - the exact call RenderService._on_tick makes at 4 Hz - announces the object's sticks key, the content-addressed hash moves e4483be4f242 to a3d6aa124df2, and the floats in the delivered geometry moved 0.4094 A; the counterexample is pinned in the same file, where an idle 1.5 s answers status 'unchanged' to the same have-hash. CONSEQUENCE APPLIED: the 20 Hz ticker WAS a second minimiser and is now a READOUT - apps/web/src/features/builder/sculptTicker.ts polls at 5 Hz with cycles 0, and cmd.sculpt_iterate(obj, state, 0) is measured to return the total strain (169.2976) while moving 0.000000 A, where 10 cycles move 0.6644 A and drop the strain to 18.9345. cmd.builder_sculpt_tick keeps its cycles argument for a backend whose pump does not idle, still reports auto_center_unsupported, and still refuses to iterate with the flag off. Every comment that repeated 'the bridge pump draws and never idles' (builder.py, BuilderPanel.tsx, controller.ts, sculptTicker.ts, p9sculpt.test.ts) is corrected in place rather than left to mislead the next reader. [wave 10 AUDIT — CONFIRMED, EVERY NUMBER REPRODUCED] This is the row shaped exactly like the two the last auditor overturned — an engine-state claim — so I re-ran it and compared the printed values digit for digit. All six match the annotation on my run: idle sculpting immediate 0.0000 A, after 2.0 s 0.6843 A, 1.0 s after `set sculpting, 0` 0.0000 A; sculpt_iterate 0 cycles -> strain 169.2976 moving 0.000000 A, 10 cycles -> strain 18.9345 moving 0.6644 A; the geometry stream hash e4483be4f242 -> a3d6aa124df2 with max vertex delta 0.4094 A, and the idle counterexample answers status=unchanged. The measurement is over SECONDS with a same-millisecond baseline read as the control, which is precisely the methodology the wave-9 row got wrong, and the counterexample (1.5 s idle, same hash) is what stops the delta being fetch non-determinism. Fixture restores sculpting, sculpting_cycles, the object list and asserts the scene came back. Client half checked: sculptTicker.ts is 200 ms / cycles 0 (`const cycles = options.cycles ?? 0`), p10sculptpoll.test.ts 3 passed. ONE LOOSENESS worth naming, not a downgrade: the stream test asserts `moved > 1e-3` where 0.4094 A was measured, and the drift test asserts `> 0.2` where 0.6843 A was measured.**

The e2e GL-free spec still passes. Mutation confirmed: removing the `picked = inst.atom2[i]; other = atom` swap turns the left/right-half test red, reverted — so 3/9 vs 9/3 is a measured property of the ray cast, not a fixture. The route registry is the same shape as shell/panelHooks (last-registered wins, unregisterable, a throwing route skipped and the pick offered on), and viewport.ts:473 really does go through dispatchViewportPick rather than straight to cmd.select, which is what makes 'a consumed hit does NOT also rewrite sele' testable without a WebGL2 context. The DOM half mounts the real BuilderPanel and asserts the 0-based -> 1-based conversion in the emitted args. The declared scope (this is the GL-free path; the server-rasterising path is PyMOL's own picking, proved in wave 8) is accurate, and the GL-free e2e spec still passes.**

A measured defect is fixed in the process: cmd.edit('pk1', <new>) DROPS pk1, so held picks are re-named by identity. Control test: with the setting off - which is the state builder_show leaves - two picks create nothing. _PKDIHE: measured to be ABSENT the instant the bond-pick RPC returns and present one draw later, because EditorDrawDihedral runs from EditorUpdate on the render loop, not from EditorActivate which only sets DihedralInvalid; it arrives with _pkdihe1/_pkdihe2 as an object:measurement. CORRECTION to the row text: which pk* selections exist depends on the pkresi flag - a multi pick (pkresi=0) builds pkset and pkmol and NOT pkresi/pkchain/pkobject; a single pick (pkresi=1) builds pkmol/pkresi/pkchain/pkobject and NOT pkset. Counted on an alanine: pkset 2 atoms, the other four 10 atoms each.**

## 11. `cmd` API surface + RPC/bridge protocol

Source doc: `docs/webclient/cmd-api-rpc.md`. This is the contract every other section calls through.

| ✔ | Feature | Source ref | Backend contract | React plan | Cx |
|---|---------|-----------|------------------|------------|----|
| [x] | **Public `cmd` API surface (404 symbols)** — `api.py` is a pure re-export manifest with no function bodies; `cmd.py:319` does `from .api import *`. AST-measured: 405 import entries, 404 unique symbols (`mpng` duplicated at `api.py:337` and `:347`). Largest contributors: editing 76, querying 63, viewing 58, moving 29, importing 25, creating 22, setting 14, commanding 13, exporting 13, fitting 13, experimenting 11, controlling 8, wizarding 8, helping 6, selecting 5, externing 5, preset 4, properties 4, colorramping 3. Plus namespace modules `cmd.util` / `cmd.movie` / `cmd.gui`, aliases `matrix_transfer=matrix_copy`, `get_setting_legacy=get_setting_float` | `modules/pymol/api.py:4-489` | every symbol reachable as `getattr(cmd, name)`; dispatcher must use a frozen allow-list built from api.py exports UNION `cmd.keyword`, minus a deny-list (system, run, spawn, quit, _quit, cd, leading-underscore) | Generated TypeScript wrapper per symbol over `client.call(m,a,k)`; tree-shakable so an app importing 2 functions ships 2 wrappers | moderate |  **[wave 5] RE-MEASURED by AST and the row is exact: 405 import entries, 404 unique, `mpng` duplicated, editing 76 / querying 63 / viewing 58. Measured from `api.py` rather than `dir(cmd)`, which would also pick up attributes, private helpers and anything a plugin has attached. The duplicate is asserted deliberately so the count stays honest \u2014 otherwise someone counting entries and someone counting `len(set(...))` disagree by one and each assumes the other is wrong. Also asserted: `api.py` contains ZERO function bodies, so there is nothing to port in it.**

| [x] | **String command execution path (`cmd.do` -> C -> `parser.parse`)** — `cmd.do` splits lines, sets defer_updates for >1 command, calls `_cmd.do` under lockcm. C invokes the Python closure `G->P_inst->parse`. `Parser._parse` handles embed sentinels, backslash continuation, semicolon splitting, leading-slash literal Python, assignment-operator sniffing via `py_delims`, kwhash abbreviation with ambiguity error, then invokes `layer.kw[0](*args,**kwargs)`. `@file` inclusion with recursion and `stop_on_exceptions`. Unknown token falls through to literal Python. Returns 1=ok, 0=exception, None=abort; **exceptions are printed, not raised** | `modules/pymol/parser.py:182-481`, do `commanding.py:441-475`, C `layer1/P.cpp:2038-2040,2352,2390` | `cmd.do(commands, log, echo, flush)` returns None and swallows errors; `parser.result` holds the last value but is NOT returned through `_cmd.do` | Envelope `t:"do"` for the console widget only; `ok` value is always null and results arrive as feedback text. **Never use for programmatic UI actions** | hard |  **[wave 5] VERIFIED, and the findings all say the same thing: a `{t:'do'}` frame is NOT one action. Semicolon splitting means one frame can hold several commands (`bg_color red; bg_color blue` runs both, last wins), so anything counting commands or driving undo cannot assume 1:1. A LEADING SLASH bypasses the keyword parser entirely and runs literal Python. And backslash continuation carries state BETWEEN FRAMES \u2014 frame N and N+1 can be two halves of one statement, so a client that treated each frame as independent (resetting UI state, or interleaving another command) would corrupt it.**

Consequence for the client: command-line arguments cannot be pre-typed, and a command whose own conversion is missing fails on its own terms rather than at the parser.**

AUTH MEASURED, and the three answers differ on purpose: `/ws` without a token is **403**, `/blob/{id}` without a token is **401**, and `/healthz` is deliberately OPEN because it carries no scene data \u2014 pinned as a decision rather than an oversight, with a test that fails if something sensitive is ever added there. The Origin allow-list is enforced (an `evil.example` Origin gets 403), which is the guard `pymolhttpd` lacks and the one a browser actually needs. `/blob/{id}` round-trips a real `get_session` payload over HTTP.**
| [x] | **Error mapping contract** — `pymol.CmdException` carries `.message` and `.label` (default `Error`), `__str__` formats as ` <label>: <message>`. `IncentiveOnlyException` subclasses it. `parsing.QuietException` is a bare Exception. The parser catches both plus SecurityException and **prints rather than propagating**. `pymolhttpd`'s `send_exception_json` already produces the right shape: message array plus traceback split into lines | `modules/pymol/__init__.py:468-480`, parser `parser.py:465-478`, shape `pymolhttpd.py:189-207` | direct cmd calls DO raise; only the parser path swallows | Map CmdException -> `{kind:'CmdException', label, message}`; QuietException -> `{kind:'QuietException'}`; anything else -> `{kind:'PythonError', traceback:[...]}` via `traceback.format_exception` | trivial |  **[wave 5] VERIFIED over the socket. A `CmdException` arrives as kind/type `CmdException` with `__str__`'s ` <label>: <message>` intact \u2014 note the LEADING SPACE is PyMOL's, so a client that strips it will also strip it from messages that lack one. The label is NOT always "Error": `IncentiveOnlyException` subclasses it and arrives as " Incentive-Only-Error: ...", so the message cannot be parsed by splitting on a literal. The two failure CLASSES are distinguishable without string parsing: an engine error carries a traceback, a policy refusal (`NotAllowed`) has an empty one, because it happens before PyMOL is called at all. And the parser really does PRINT rather than propagate \u2014 `{t:'do'}` of a bad command returns an OK frame with the complaint on the feedback topic, so a client surfacing errors only from `err` frames would show the user nothing.**

## 12. Build & tooling

Source doc: `docs/webclient/build-and-tooling.md`.

> **Reconciled (WP-27, wave 4).** The source map was transmitted truncated at feature 2 of 22 and
> this area was held provisional pending re-reading it. Done, and the conclusion is that the area
> is correctly TWO rows, not 22.
>
> `build-and-tooling.md` is a build/architecture reference, not a list of front-end obligations: its
> sections are the PEP 517 chain, a Homebrew dependency audit, measured build timings, a catalogue of
> macOS arm64 failure modes, a **proposed** monorepo layout (not the one adopted — the tree uses
> `apps/`, `packages/`, `bridge/` at the root, not `webclient/`), file contents for that proposal, and
> CI. The `features[]` framing came from the mapper's return schema, which required an array; almost
> none of those entries are user-facing PyMOL features that a React client must reproduce, which is
> what this inventory counts.
>
> The two rows below are the only build facts a client author can get wrong, and both are now
> verified rather than asserted. The 351 total is no longer provisional on this area.

| ✔ | Feature | Source ref | Backend contract | React plan | Cx |
|---|---------|-----------|------------------|------------|----|
| [x] | **pip/setup.py build path (the only real build)** — `pip install .` drives a PEP 517 backend that runs setup.py, which generates shader/buildinfo headers, computes all compiler flags, then shells out to CMake. Verified working on darwin/arm64: clean wheel build = 15.4 s wall / 111 s user on M4 Max -j16, 254 object files, 9.3 MB `_cmd.cpython-313-darwin.so`, 7.5 MB wheel, version 3.2.0a0 | `setup.py:880-888`, `pyproject.toml:20-27` | produces `pymol._cmd` (CPython extension) and `chempy.champ._champ` (`setup.py:860-878`). `_cmd._new/_start/_stop/_idle/_getRedisplay/_reshape/_draw/_button/_drag` are the C entry points the bridge sits on | Not cloned in React. Wrapped by `webclient/scripts/bootstrap.sh` pinning the interpreter, PREFIX_PATH and config-settings so every dev gets a byte-identical build | moderate **[wave 4] VERIFIED: `scripts/bootstrap.sh` drives exactly this path and builds PyMOL from this tree into `bridge/.venv` — 34 s cold, 6 s idempotent, 254 object files. `pnpm run doctor` confirms `PyMOL 3.2.0a Open-Source (c969b5fa09)` importing from the venv with MMTF/BCIF live (877-byte round trip) and an offscreen GL context. Recipe and the one real failure (vendoring mmtf-cpp headers, `--config-settings use-msgpackc=c++11`) are in `spikes/00-build.md`.** |
| [x] | **`CMakeLists.txt` is a stub, not a standalone build** — 31 lines whose every variable (`TARGET_NAME`, `ALL_SRC`, `ALL_INC_DIR`, `ALL_DEF`, `ALL_LIB`, `ALL_LIB_DIR`, `ALL_COMP_ARGS`, `ALL_EXT_LINK`, `SHARED_SUFFIX`) is injected by `setup.py` as `-D` flags. CMake is never invoked standalone; the CMake path IS the pip path | `CMakeLists.txt:1-31`, injection at `setup.py:381-393` | none — this is a build-system fact, not a runtime API | Nothing to port. The web client must never document a CMake-only build, and `scripts/bootstrap.sh` must stay the single entry point **[wave 4] RECONCILED (B5, the row was transmitted truncated) and VERIFIED: copying `CMakeLists.txt` alone into an empty directory and running `cmake .` fails with `target_link_libraries called with incorrect number of arguments` / `Configuring incomplete, errors occurred!`. Confirmed against `setup.py:382,386`.** | trivial |

## 13. Not achievable client-side

The verdict below is the geometry-extraction agent's, grounded in the files it read. These are the features the "render client-side in WebGL, PyMOL only serializes geometry" decision puts at risk. **Every one of them needs an explicit product decision before implementation starts.** They are cross-referenced with ⛔ in the tables above.

### 13.1 Hard-impossible in the browser — must be server-rendered or dropped

| Feature | Why | Source ref | Only viable path |
|---------|-----|-----------|------------------|
| **Ray tracing (`cmd.ray`) will never match** | `cmd.ray` drives a 7827-line CPU ray tracer with true shadows, interior colors, per-primitive analytic intersection and `ray_trace_mode` cel-shading/outlines. `ray_trace_mode` **does not exist in the GL path at all** — grep of `layer1/SceneRender.cpp` and `layer1/SceneRay.cpp` returned zero hits. There is no three.js path to parity | `layer1/Ray.cpp` (whole file), cel-shading `:5570-5607,6472-6474` | The web client **must** call `cmd.ray()` + `cmd.png()` server-side and display the bitmap. This is a hard product constraint, not an implementation detail |
| **`cRepCallback` objects** | Created by `cmd.load_callback`, they execute arbitrary user Python issuing raw OpenGL calls at render time. No CGO, no vertex array, no primitive list — by construction there is nothing to serialize | `layer1/Rep.h:65`, `layer2/ObjectCallback.cpp` | Declare unsupported with an explicit marker from the accessor, or fall back to a server-side pixel stream for scenes containing one |
| **Quad-buffer / stencil-parity / OpenVR stereo** | PyMOL supports quadbuffer, crosseye, walleye, geowall, sidebyside, stencil-by-row/column/checkerboard/custom, anaglyph and openvr. Quad-buffer and stencil-parity stereo have no WebGL equivalent; OpenVR is out of scope | `layer1/SceneRay.cpp:154-175,515-713`, menu `modules/pymol/_gui.py:420-433` | Keep only anaglyph and side-by-side. Reject the rest with a clear feedback message rather than silently doing nothing |
| **GPU colour-index picking in the browser** | `SceneDoXYPick` renders a dedicated pick pass in the **backend** GL context and `PyMOLReadPixels` reads back one pixel. A headless backend needs an offscreen/EGL context and every pick costs a full render | `layer1/ScenePicking.cpp:17-38,146-149` | Picking stays backend-authoritative. Provision an offscreen GL context. Any client-side camera prediction that leaks into a pick makes users select the wrong atom with no error |
| **`pymol.cmd._call_with_opengl_context`** | Makes the BACKEND depend on a current OpenGL context living in the GUI process. With rendering in three.js there is no GL context in Python; anything routed through this hook (shader-dependent ops, some ray/draw/png paths) breaks | `modules/pmg_qt/pymol_qt_gui.py:1245-1252`, users `exporting.py:499-602`, `moving.py:366-434` | Offscreen GL context in the bridge, or fail loudly with a named error. `cmd.draw` explicitly does not work headless (`viewing.py:1630-1632`) |
| **STL export** | `get_stlstr` unconditionally raises `IncentiveOnlyException('STL export not supported by this PyMOL build')`. No C++ implementation exists in the open-source tree | `modules/pymol/lazyio.py:224-231` | Hide the option or surface the exception verbatim |
| **`.MTL` export and OBJ fidelity** | `.MTL` raises `CmdException('.MTL export not implemented')`; OBJ writes spheres as degenerate three-identical-vertex faces, emits no cylinders at all, and has vertex colors commented out | `modules/pymol/exporting.py:981-986`, `layer1/Ray.cpp:2430-2506` | Remove from the UI or ship clearly labelled broken |
| **`cmd.get_gltf`** | A shell-out to an external `collada2gltf` binary that raises if missing. No PyMOL-side glTF code exists (grep-verified) | `modules/pymol/querying.py:664-690` | If glTF is a requirement, write a new Python serializer on the NEW binary geometry accessor — CGO_DRAW_ARRAYS maps to glTF accessors almost verbatim |

### 13.2 Achievable but will visibly differ — accept, or pay for parity

| Feature | Why it differs | Source ref |
|---------|----------------|-----------|
| **Transparency** | `transparency_mode 3` uses a multi-draw-buffer OIT accumulation FBO with a dedicated shader. Other modes CPU-sort triangles **every frame** (`CGOOptimizeToVBOIndexedWithColorEmbedTransparentInfo` embeds z_value/ix/sort_mem; `RepSurface::render` re-sorts NT triangles per frame). Per-frame sorting of 10^6 triangles in JS will not hold 60 fps | `layer1/SceneRender.cpp:939-995`, `layer1/CGO.cpp:3395-3405`, `layer2/RepSurface.cpp:1969-1984`, shaders `data/shaders/oit.vs/.fs` |
| **Post-process anti-aliasing** | `antialias_shader` selects FXAA (1 stage) or SMAA (3 stages). Porting SMAA with PyMOL's exact parameters is nontrivial; edges will visibly differ at high zoom | `layer1/SceneRender.cpp:613-627,1791` |
| **Labels** | Label geometry is texture-atlas quads (CGO_DRAW_LABEL/CGO_DRAW_LABELS) with a dynamic texture-size heuristic. Glyph bitmaps are CPU-reachable but browser rasterization will not byte-match PyMOL's FreeType output | `layer1/CGO.h:224-228`, `layer2/RepLabel.cpp:114-117`, `layer1/Character.cpp:122` |
| **Volume and slice reps** | Ray-marched 3D textures, not meshes. Portable in principle (`Data3DTexture` + a `volume.fs` port) but a separate epic; the ray-volume interop path has no browser analogue | `layer1/Rep.h:66-71`, `layer1/SceneRay.cpp:773-816`, `data/shaders/volume.fs` |
| **Grid mode layout** | `GridMode::ByObject / ByObjectStates / ByObjectByState` split the viewport into N slots with an aspect adjustment. Approximating `GridUpdate` produces visibly misaligned panels | `layer1/SceneRay.cpp:179-211,306-320` |
| **Frame-rate readout and the busy box** | Both depend on the render-loop timing (`ButModeSetRate`) which will not exist in the same form once rendering moves to three.js | `layer1/ButMode.cpp:98-122` |
| **SDOF / spaceball input** | No browser equivalent short of WebHID; recommend explicitly dropping | `layer1/Control.cpp:83-216`, `layer4/Cmd.cpp:3665` |
| **Tear-off menus** | `setTearOffEnabled(True)` on every Qt menu and in `PmwMenuBar`. No browser equivalent | `modules/pmg_qt/pymol_qt_gui.py:297`, `mimic_pmg_tk.py:90` |

### 13.3 Extraction hazards that can silently corrupt the feed

| Hazard | Detail | Source ref |
|--------|--------|-----------|
| **VBO readback is impossible** | `OptimizeVertsToVBONotIndexed` builds a stack-local `std::vector<float>`, uploads it, and drops it. The header documents it: "the geometry data is not kept in the CGO object for VBO objects (only on the card)". The accessor MUST read the pre-VBO CGO (primitiveCGO / preshader / ray) or the RepSurface vectors, **never** the render CGO | `layer1/CGO.cpp:2947-2998`, `layer1/CGO.h:183-186` |
| **Cartoon geometry is destroyed at first GL render** | `RepCartoonCGOGenerate` calls `disposePreshaderCGO`; if `I->ray` is non-null the preshader is freed outright. In a GUI-attached process the accessor may find both preshader and std unusable. Same hazard for `RepSphere::renderCGO` aliasing primitiveCGO | `layer2/RepCartoon.cpp:83-89,240`, `layer2/RepSphere.cpp:68-71` |
| **Thread safety** | `Rep::update()` runs on worker threads when `async_builds` and `max_threads>1`. Any new invalidation callback into Python must use PAutoBlock/PAutoUnblock exactly as RepSurface's cache hook does, and the accessor must hold the API lock or it will race a rebuild and read freed vectors | `layer1/Scene.cpp:4700-4757`, pattern `layer2/RepSurface.cpp:4569-4581`, lock pattern `layer4/Cmd.cpp:2047-2053` |
| **Picking encoding must be replicated bit-for-bit** | CGO_PICK_COLOR carries (atom index, bond index) with sentinels cPickableAtom -1 through cPickableThrough -5. Any mismatch in packing means selections silently land on the wrong atoms — a correctness bug that looks like a UI bug. **The pick-color sub-block byte layout was NOT verified at runtime** and must be confirmed against a live CGO | `layer1/CGO.h:141-142`, `modules/pymol/cgo.py:73-77`, layout `layer1/CGO.cpp:60-64,1664-1666` |
| **No existing export path is usable as a feed** | All six geometry exporters call SceneRay, which does SceneUpdate, allocates a fresh CRay, re-renders every object into ray primitives, expands and transforms them, sprintf's ASCII, then frees everything. No caching, no dirty tracking, no incremental mode. Order 1-10 s for VRML, 10-100 s for COLLADA. **The project therefore DEPENDS on new C++ being written; there is no zero-backend-change fallback** | `layer1/SceneRay.cpp:88,152,213,284-323,492`, `layer1/Ray.cpp:561,822` |
| **Surface cache feed is colourless and evictable** | The cached SurfaceJob result is only (N, V, VN, NT, T, S) — it omits VC, VA, Vis, VAO and AT, which live on the RepSurface and not the job. `pymol._cache` is also size- and LRU-evicted. Prototype only | `layer2/RepSurface.cpp:3217-3229`, `modules/pymol/internal.py:48-78` |
| **COLLADA requires libxml2 at build time** | Gated on `_HAVE_LIBXML`. Without it `RayRenderCOLLADA` emits an error and returns nothing, which also breaks `get_gltf` since it is built on `get_collada` | `setup.py:631-633`, `layer1/COLLADA.cpp:681-684` |

## 14. Required new backend APIs (the gap list)

Nothing in §3, §7 (timeline, sequence viewer) or §11 (events) can be built without these. They are **backend work**, not React work, and they are the critical path.

| # | New API | Needed by | Source anchor / precedent |
|---|---------|-----------|---------------------------|
| 1 | `_cmd.get_rep_geometry(name, state, rep)` returning PyBytes blobs per array | §3 all rep rows | `PConvFloatArrayToPyList(..., dump_binary=true)` `layer1/PConv.cpp:971-977`; walk pattern `CGOArrayAsPyList` `layer1/CGO.cpp:241-287`; lock pattern `layer4/Cmd.cpp:2047-2053` |
| 2 | Rep invalidation callback (object, state, rep, level) fired from `Rep::invalidate`/`update` | §3 incremental updates — without it the client re-pulls everything on every change | `layer1/Rep.h:133-184`; hook pattern `call_raw_image_callback` `layer1/Scene.cpp:4020-4051`; GIL pattern `layer2/RepSurface.cpp:4569-4581` |
| 3 | Panel snapshot endpoint: names + type + enabled + reps + color + group_name + nest_level + group open state + caption | §2 object panel — everything except nest_level and open-state exists; this is the single blocking API gap for the panel | `layer3/ExecutiveDef.h:20-31`; partial data via `cmd.get_session(partial=1)['names'][i][6]` and `ObjectGroupAsPyList` `layer2/ObjectGroup.cpp:60-73` |
| 4 | `get_movie_panel()` — per-frame `specification_level` arrays per row plus scene/state flags | §2 / §7 movie timeline | `MovieGetSpecLevel` `layer1/Movie.cpp:163`, `ObjectGetSpecLevel` `layer1/PyMOLObject.cpp:109-121` — C-only, absent from the `_cmd` table |
| 5 | `get_seq_view()` — rows of cells with text/offset/color/selected/spacer/unaligned/state/tag/atomIndices, plus a `seq_changed` event | §7 sequence viewer. `cmd.get_fastastr` is not a substitute (no colors, atom indices, selection state, gaps or alignment offsets) | `layer3/Seeker.cpp:969,1947`; dirty hook `layer1/Seq.cpp:136` |
| 6 | `cmd.get_click_string` Python wrapper | §4 click-ready payload; the `clik`/SimpleClick pathway is unusable from the bridge today | C exists at `layer4/Cmd.cpp:1420-1436,6451`; **zero Python callers** |
| 7 | `ButModeGet` / `ButModeTranslate` readable from Python | §4 mouse-config UI and pointerdown gesture classification | `layer1/ButMode.h:225,227` — C-only; only `cmd.button` (write) exists in `modules/` |
| 8 | ButMode 5-character action code table accessor | §2 ButMode grid | `layer1/ButMode.cpp:497-520+` — C-only, no Python accessor |
| 9 | Setting default / min / max / help accessor | §5 advanced settings table (Default column, Reset, clamping, tooltips) | `layer1/SettingInfo.h:45-57`; `data/setting_help.csv` (875 rows, **zero consumers**); precedent `CmdGetSettingLevel` `layer4/Cmd.cpp:4403` |
| 10 | `cmd.get_setting_level` Python wrapper | §5 scope gating | C exists at `layer4/Cmd.cpp:4403`, not wrapped in `modules/pymol/setting.py` |
| 11 | Wizard-changed push (bridge wrapping `refresh_wizard`/`set_wizard`/`dirty_wizard`) | §8 every wizard panel | `layer1/Wizard.cpp:195` — C pulls from Python; **no observer hook exists** |
| 12 | Editor-state push `{pk1..pk4, bondMode, nFrag}` after every change | §9 builder branching, §8 wizard picks | `layer3/Editor.cpp:499-536,1786-1830` |
| 13 | Recent-files RPC pair wrapping `_gui.recent_filenames` / `recent_filenames_add` | §1 Open Recent | `modules/pymol/_gui.py:975-1032` |
| 14 | `movie.capabilities()` wrapping `pymol.movie.find_exe` | §6 / §7 movie export dialog | `modules/pymol/movie.py:824-844` |
| 15 | `mdump`-equivalent structured getter (per-frame movie command strings) | §7 movie command editor | `cmd.mdump` only prints (`moving.py:81`, `layer1/Movie.cpp:378`) |
| 16 | Iterate-with-`space` server-side helper (run the iterate, return the namespace) | §10 Properties Inspector | `layer1/P.cpp:455-606`; caller `properties_dialog.py:350-357` |
| 17 | Change events: `volume_ramp_changed`, `scenes_changed`, `setting_changed`, `pk1_changed`, `objects_changed`, `view_changed`, `progress` | §10, §5, §2, §7, §11 — replaces paint-event polling and direct widget callbacks | `colorramping.py:170-179` (direct widget call today), `scene_bin_gui.py:102-113` (paint polling today) |
| 18 | `get_qtwindow()`-equivalent shim implementing `window_cmd` | §1 `cmd.window`, `pymol.gui.save_as`/`save_image` | `modules/pymol/viewing.py:1446-1457`, `modules/pymol/gui.py:70-92` |
| 19 | `pymol.cmd._copy_image`, `_call_in_gui_thread`, `_call_with_opengl_context`, `pymol.gui.createlegacypmgapp` filled by the bridge | §1 startup; the backend calls into the GUI through these four seams | `modules/pmg_qt/pymol_qt_gui.py:1245-1252` |
| 20 | Blocking-dialog bridge (suspend a Python thread for a client round-trip) | §6 `ask_partial` (`exec()`), the tkinter filedialog shim, any plugin modal | `file_dialogs.py:88`, `mimic_tk.py:36-90` |

## 15. Global risks

Ordered by how much of the product they can take down.

1. **There is no event bus in the C++ core.** `grep -r Notify` over layer0..layer5 returns zero hits. Every "live" surface in the current GUI is a poll. Without Tier-1 snapshot diffing plus Tier-2 change counters, the React client will either be stale or will hammer the backend. (`layer1/Ortho.cpp:492-515`, `layer3/Executive.cpp:313-322`)
2. **No geometry accessor exists.** All six existing exporters go through SceneRay (full re-render, ASCII, 1-100 s) and are individually lossy. **The project depends on new C++ being written.** (`layer1/SceneRay.cpp:88`, §13.3)
3. **Picking must stay backend-authoritative and needs an offscreen GL context.** Every pick is a full render + `glReadPixels` in the backend context. (`layer1/ScenePicking.cpp:17-38`)
4. **Ray tracing has no client-side path.** Ray output must be a server-rendered bitmap. (`layer1/Ray.cpp`)
5. **`OrthoFeedbackIn` is gated on `G->Option->pmgui`.** A headless bridge gets zero console output. Verify empirically before committing to the polling design. (`layer1/Ortho.cpp:492-499`)
6. **Consume-once drain APIs.** `cmd.get_setting_updates()` clears flags as it reads and `cmd._get_feedback()` pops one string per call. Exactly one consumer may exist; the bridge must own both and fan out, and any surviving Qt window must be disabled. (`layer1/Setting.cpp:1121-1147`, `modules/pymol/internal.py:593-606`)
7. **`cmd.do` swallows return values and exceptions.** Any UI feature built on the `do` path is blind to failure and always receives null. (`modules/pymol/parser.py:465-481`, `commanding.py:441-475`)
8. **Executing menu/wizard/panel command strings over a WebSocket is remote code execution against the local process.** It is exactly what `PopUp.cpp:471-475` does today and is unavoidable for parity, but it must never be exposed on a non-loopback socket, and both legacy bridges (`rpc.py:441` `register_instance(cmd)`, `pymolhttpd.py:63-67`) show how easy it is to get wrong.
9. **LITERAL1/LITERAL2 commands eval user strings** — alter, iterate, alter_state, iterate_state, label, alias, set_key. `pymolhttpd` already dodged label by substituting `label2`. Full desktop parity means shipping arbitrary Python execution to a browser. (`modules/pymol/keywords.py:16,19,21,144-147,257`)
10. **Browser keyboard hijacking.** PyMOL binds CTRL-T (bond;unpick) and CTRL-F (wizard find); CTRL-W/CTRL-T/CTRL-N cannot be `preventDefault`-ed in most browsers. Ctrl+wheel is trackpad pinch in Chrome/Firefox, colliding with Ctrl+wheel = mvsz. Middle/right click need `preventDefault` on auxclick/contextmenu.
11. **Timing-based input semantics are measured backend-side** (0.35 s double-click, 0.25 s single-click window, 0.15 s delay, 4 px / 10 px thresholds) against `UtilGetSeconds`. Network jitter inflates the measured press->release gap and will silently swallow single clicks unless client timestamps are passed through as `when`. (`layer1/SceneMouse.cpp:26,664-674`, `layer1/Scene.cpp:4113-4155`)
12. **Settings are not cosmetic.** `SettingGenerateSideEffects` invalidates reps and forces geometry rebuilds, so almost any `cmd.set` may require a fresh serialized geometry payload. (`layer1/Setting.cpp:1872-1930`)
13. **Broken-in-this-build features that the UI still advertises:** `cmd.clean` (IncentiveOnly, breaks the Builder Clean button and CleanWizard); `editor.undocontext` is a no-op stub so most "undoable" Builder actions are not undoable; `cmd.push_undo` only partly implemented; mae load, `load_mtz`, vis, moe, phypo raise IncentiveOnly; `.MTL` export raises; STL raises; `pmo` is rejected. (`computing.py:20-29`, `editor.py:38-49`, `editing.py:531-546`, `importing.py:31-33,1511,1620,1641-1643`, `exporting.py:641-642,981-986`, `lazyio.py:224-231`)
14. **Dead code and latent bugs that a faithful clone would reproduce:** `editor.combine_monomer` / `combine_nucleotide` do not exist (AttributeError if the panel entry is added); `setUndoEnabled` passes a literal `[N more]` pseudo-name to `cmd.unset`; `data/chempy/fragments/utpB.pkl` is missing; duplicate `double_left` rows in three_button_motions and two_button_selecting; missing `break` in `SceneClickPickNothing`; double release dispatch in `OrthoButton`; `ButModeSet(cButModeMiddleCtSh)` written twice ("SET TWICE?!?" in the source); `shortcut_menu_gui.itemChanged` only writes the description when no filter is active; Delete Selected iterates `selectedIndexes()` per cell not per row; the PNG dialog's size fields and `PyMOLMapLoad.py` are unreferenced; `colorramping.ramp_to_colors` uses Python-2 integer division and raises on Python 3.
15. **`cmd.set_color` auto-detects 0-255 vs 0-1 by "any component > 1.0"**, so a legitimate `[1,0,0]` in 0-255 space becomes red-in-0-1. The web picker must always send 0-1 floats. (`modules/pymol/viewing.py:2205-2207`)
16. **Menus hardcode generated color-table indices** (134, 104, 4236, 74) computed from the ColorReset layout. `set_color` on a generated name or `cmd.space` makes those labels lie. (`modules/pymol/_gui.py:456-461,632,741`, `layer1/Color.cpp:825-1322`)
17. **Legacy Tk plugins cannot run in the browser.** `mimic_pmg_tk.PMGApp` creates a real hidden `tkinter.Tk()` pumped by a QTimer, and `mimic_tk.py` installs a **global** `sys.meta_path` hook that will still fire in a headless backend, handing plugins Qt dialogs nobody can see. (`mimic_pmg_tk.py:140-168`, `mimic_tk.py:128`)
18. **`New PyMOL Window` spawns a whole OS process via `os.spawnv`**, contradicting the one-process/one-client architecture, and is reachable from two places (File menu and the partial-session dialog). (`modules/pymol/_gui.py:41-53`)
19. **A browser cannot resize its own window**, but `pymolviewport` resizes the WINDOW to satisfy `cmd.viewport w, h`. `cmd.viewport` must instead resize the canvas and report the achieved size back — observably different for scripts. (`modules/pmg_qt/pymol_qt_gui.py:62-81`)
20. **Shutdown has no safe hook.** `confirm_quit` does not confirm anything and `closeEvent` calls `cmd.quit()` unconditionally. A browser tab close cannot reliably run this; the bridge needs a heartbeat/lifecycle policy and an unsaved-session guard. (`pymol_qt_gui.py:56-57,875-876`)
21. **Clipboard operations require a user gesture and a secure context**, so backend-initiated clipboard writes (`cmd.get_view` triggered from a script, `_copy_image`) will silently fail. (`pymol_qt_gui.py:83-86,1170-1185`)
22. **`data/pmg_qt/styles/pymol.sty` has only 3 rules.** Almost all visual identity comes from the native Qt theme, so the React shell has **no design source of truth to copy** and needs a real design pass.
23. **Large payloads (PSE, CCP4 maps, MP4, geometry blobs) must stream over HTTP**, not the WebSocket JSON channel.
24. **Server-side chdir and subprocess side effects.** `movie.produce` chdir's the process; `cmd.system`, `cmd.run`, `@`, `cd` are full local code execution; the Text Editor and filter wizard write arbitrary files. Acceptable only under the stated localhost assumption, and must be surfaced to the user, not silently executed from a browser click.

---

## 16. Open decisions needed from the product owner

Grouped; each blocks at least one work package.

**Architecture / scope**

1. Does the backend get new C++ (geometry accessor + invalidation callback + change counters), or is C++ frozen? If frozen, the WebGL viewport as specified is not buildable — see §13.3 "No existing export path is usable as a feed".
2. Which stereo modes survive? Recommendation: anaglyph + side-by-side only; reject quadbuffer/byrow/openvr with a feedback message.
3. Is `cRepCallback` supported at all, or explicitly out of scope? Is volume/slice rendering v1 or a later epic?
4. Is the movie timeline in scope for v1? It is the most expensive single surface (per-frame keyframe data + 8 modifier-dependent drag modes + a new backend accessor).
5. Is client-side predictive camera worth building given localhost RTT is ~1-3 ms? Recommendation: full round-trip by default, prediction only for rota/move/movz behind a flag.
6. Who owns the movie clock — the backend `SceneIdle` loop (current behaviour) or the browser rAF loop?
7. For scene recall with `animate>0`: does the client tween locally, or does the server stream interpolated views? Server-side matches `ViewElemInterpolate` exactly but costs one message per frame.
8. Is a headless GL context available in the target deployment? `cmd.draw` does not work without one, which would force `mode=ray` for all movie export.

**Product behaviour**

9. `File > New PyMOL Window` and the partial-session dialog's "Open in new PyMOL Window": disable, or spawn a second backend on another port and open a new tab?
10. `Working Directory > File Browser` (`cmd.system 'open .'`): drop, or re-target at the in-app picker?
11. Should single-file exports default to a browser download (`get_bytes`/`get_str`/`png`) or to writing a server path? A mixed model needs a clear default per format.
12. Where do browser uploads land — cwd, or a dedicated `~/.pymol/uploads` scratch dir? This changes the implicit `cd` behaviour of Run Script.
13. Is `.pwg` refused outright, or supported behind an explicit confirmation?
14. Are legacy Tk plugins in scope at all? Option (a) drop them and define a new React plugin API; option (b) keep them headless and have them register JSON menu descriptors over the bridge.
15. Is the Text Editor in scope as an in-browser file editor with full filesystem access, or replaced by a pymolrc-only settings surface?
16. Is SDOF/spaceball in scope via WebHID, or explicitly dropped?
17. Should the web build remap PyMOL defaults that collide with unblockable browser shortcuts (CTRL-T, CTRL-F, CTRL-N), or require an installed PWA / explicit keyboard-capture mode?

**Fidelity vs. correctness**

18. Do we clone the source's bugs for byte-level fidelity, or fix them and maintain a documented divergence list? Concrete cases: swapped singular/plural wizard prompts, the `Chlorrine` and `Sulfer` typos, `Attaching Amino Acid` on the nucleic-acid wizard, the `[N more]` unset bug, duplicate mouse-mode rows, the missing `break` in `SceneClickPickNothing`, the shortcut-menu proxy-index bug.
19. Radio menu groups: keyed per-setting (faithful — groups for the same setting in different submenus share one exclusive group) or per-submenu (saner)? Behaviour differs.
20. `_addmenu` silently drops items whose command is `None`. Does a serialized JSON menu tree mean omitted, or disabled?
21. `cmd.clean` is incentive-only. Ship the Clean button disabled, or wire an open-source MMFF94 minimizer behind the same `cmd.clean(selection, message=..., async_=1)` signature?
22. `undocontext` is a no-op, so most "undoable" Builder actions are not undoable. Implement a real undo context on the bridge (snapshot + restore), or ship at parity with open-source PyMOL?
23. Should the web client keep `internal_gui_width` / `internal_gui_control_size` / `internal_gui_mode` / `seq_view_location` / `seq_view_overlay` as real PyMOL settings (session round-trip parity) or replace them with CSS-only layout?
24. Should `Display > Internal GUI / Internal Prompt / Internal Feedback / Overlay` be exposed at all, given the internal ortho GUI is being replaced by React components?
25. Wizard panels are drawn by PyMOL INSIDE the GL viewport. Clone that placement (floating overlay anchored to the canvas) for fidelity, or promote wizard panels into the React side panel for usability?
26. Should the Scene Panel expose the real scene message (`get_scene_message`/`set_scene_message`) and an actions column, which the Qt panel stubs with placeholders?
27. Should the 5200 generated colors (s000..., r000..., c000..., w000..., o000..., grey/gray NN) appear in the picker, or stay hidden behind an "advanced" toggle as `cmd.get_color_indices()` already does?
28. Where does persistence live: `~/.pymol/recent.db`, `~/.pymol/shortcuts_save.json` (server-side, faithful, survives storage clears) or browser storage?
29. `seq_view_format` value 5 ("movie frames") is declared but is an empty no-op and is not in the menu. Implement or drop?
30. What is the payload budget for `get_seq_view()` on ribosome-scale structures? Needs a protocol-level chunking/virtualization decision, not just a render-level one.
